# Weak and strong normalization in pure type systems

Coordinate `EN-106-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M1-B-AL-H-0508` | `PM-245.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Weak-and-strong-normalization-in-pure-type-systems-September-25-2026` | date 2026-09-25 | derived from the openai/math preprint Weak-and-strong-normalization-in-pure-type-systems-September-25-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). ```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that every weakly <i>β</i>-normalizing pure type system is strongly <i>β</i>-normalizing. Both properties quantify over all legal expressions in all valid contexts, and reduction acts inside type annotations. No functionality hypothesis is required. This resolves the <i>β</i>-Barendregt–Geuvers–Klop conjecture.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every weakly <i>β</i>-normalizing pure type system is strongly <i>β</i>-normalizing. Both properties quantify over all legal expressions in all valid contexts, and reduction acts inside type annotations. No functionality hypothesis is required. This resolves the <i>β</i>-Barendregt–Geuvers–Klop conjecture.

- **claim-system-weak-implies-strong** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $\mathcal P=(\mathcal S,\mathcal A,\mathcal R)$ be any pure type system. If every legal expression of $\mathcal P$, in every valid context, is weakly $\beta$-normalizing, then every such expression is strongly $\beta$-normalizing. No functionality assumption is imposed on $\mathcal A$ or $\mathcal R$.
  - Explanation: System-wide weak beta normalization implies strong beta normalization for legal expressions in arbitrary valid contexts, including annotations, without functional axiom or product relations.
- **claim-context-validity-and-scope** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pts-validity)): If $\Gamma\vdash M:A$, then $\Gamma$ is valid, and $\mathrm{FV}(M)\cup\mathrm{FV}(A)\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(\Gamma)$. For each declaration of $\Gamma$, its sorting judgment in the preceding prefix occurs as a subderivation of the given derivation.
  - Explanation: A derivable judgment supplies a valid context, scopes both expressions, and retains the sorting subderivations for its declarations.
- **claim-thinning-by-insertion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pts-thinning)): Suppose $\Gamma\vdash M:A$, and suppose that $\Delta$ is a valid context containing the declarations of $\Gamma$ unchanged and in their original order. Then $\Delta\vdash M:A$.
  - Explanation: Valid insertion of declarations preserves the original subject and expected type when the original declarations retain their order and contents.
- **claim-typed-substitution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pts-substitution)): Let $\Gamma=(x_1:A_1,\ldots,x_n:A_n)$ and let $\Delta$ be valid. Suppose a simultaneous substitution $\sigma$ satisfies $$\Delta\vdash\sigma(x_i):A_i[\sigma\|_{\{x_1,\ldots,x_{i-1}\}}] \qquad(1\leq i\leq n).$$ Then $\Gamma\vdash M:A$ implies $\Delta\vdash M\sigma:A\sigma$. In particular, if $$\Gamma,x:D,\Theta\vdash M:A, \qquad \Gamma\vdash N:D,$$ then $$\Gamma,\Theta[x:=N]\vdash M[x:=N]:A[x:=N].$$
  - Explanation: A substitution respecting the dependent declaration types transports typing, including substitution through a dependent context suffix.
- **claim-convertible-declaration-replacement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pts-context-conversion)): Suppose $\Gamma\vdash D:s$, $\Gamma\vdash D':s'$, and $D=_\beta D'$. Replacing $x:D$ by $x:D'$ in any valid context $\Gamma,x:D,\Theta$ preserves validity and every derivable judgment, with the subject, expected type, and later declarations unchanged. The converse replacement has the same property.
  - Explanation: Replacing a declaration type by a sorted convertible type preserves validity and typing in both directions, even when their sorting judgments use different sorts.
- **claim-typing-generation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pts-generation)): For derivable judgments in an arbitrary pure type system:* 1. *If $\Gamma\vdash x:T$, then $x:D$ occurs in $\Gamma$ and $T=_\beta D$.* 2. *If $\Gamma\vdash s:T$, then $(s,u)\in\mathcal A$ and $T=_\beta u$ for some sort $u$.* 3. *If $\Gamma\vdash\Pi x:D.E:T$, then for some $(a,b,c)\in\mathcal R$, $$\Gamma\vdash D:a,\qquad \Gamma,x:D\vdash E:b,\qquad T=_\beta c.$$* 4. *If $\Gamma\vdash\lambda x:D.M:T$, then for some $E,c$, $$\Gamma,x:D\vdash M:E,\qquad \Gamma\vdash\Pi x:D.E:c,\qquad T=_\beta\Pi x:D.E.$$* 5. *If $\Gamma\vdash MN:T$, then for some $D,E,x$, $$\Gamma\vdash M:\Pi x:D.E,\qquad \Gamma\vdash N:D,\qquad T=_\beta E[x:=N].$$* *Every immediate syntactic child of a typable expression is typable in the corresponding context, with a binder declaration added for a body or codomain.
  - Explanation: Generation recovers the premises determined by each syntactic constructor, modulo conversion, and types every immediate child in its corresponding context.
- **claim-correctness-of-types** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pts-correctness)): If $\Gamma\vdash M:A$, then either $A$ is a literal sort or $\Gamma\vdash A:s$ for some sort $s$. Moreover, every type assigned to an application is sorted, including an assigned type that is itself a literal sort. Consequently every nonliteral legal expression is typable.
  - Explanation: Expected types are literal sorts or sorted expressions; application result types are sorted even when literal, so nonliteral legal expressions are typable.
- **claim-confluence-and-product-compatibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pts-confluence)): Full beta reduction on raw expressions is confluent. Therefore distinct sorts are not convertible, no product is convertible to a sort, and $$\Pi x:D.E=_\beta\Pi x:D'.E' \quad\Longrightarrow\quad D=_\beta D'\ \hbox{ and }\ E=_\beta E',$$ after consistently renaming the bound variables.
  - Explanation: Raw full beta reduction is confluent, separates products from sorts and distinct sorts from one another, and identifies corresponding components of convertible products.
- **claim-subject-reduction-exact-type** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pts-subject-reduction)): If $\Gamma\vdash M:A$ and $M\to_\beta^*M'$, then $\Gamma\vdash M':A$, with the exact original expected type.
  - Explanation: Full beta reduction preserves a derivable judgment at its exact original expected type.
- **claim-neutral-type-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pts-neutral-uniqueness)): If $H$ is neutral and $\Gamma\vdash H:A$ and $\Gamma\vdash H:B$, then $A=_\beta B$. If $\Gamma\vdash H:s$ for a literal sort $s$, then $s$ itself is sorted in $\Gamma$. Every normal typable application is neutral.
  - Explanation: Neutral expressions have unique types modulo conversion even in nonfunctional systems; their literal sort types are sorted, and normal typable applications are neutral.
- **claim-normalizing-judgments** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-pts-normalizing-judgments)): Assume that every legal expression is weakly normalizing. Write $M^\#$ for its normal form, uniquely determined up to alpha equivalence. If $\Gamma\vdash M:A$, then $$\Gamma\vdash M^\#:A \qquad\text{and}\qquad \Gamma\vdash M^\#:A^\#.$$ The second judgment is also available with $M$ in place of $M^\#$. If $\Gamma\vdash A:s$, then $\Gamma\vdash A^\#:s$. A sorted normal expression is either a sort, a neutral expression, or a product.
  - Explanation: Under system-wide weak normalization, subjects and expected types may be normalized while retaining typing; sorted normal expressions have the stated syntactic classification.
- **claim-finite-counterexample-restriction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-pts-finite-restriction)): Let $\mathcal P=(\mathcal S,\mathcal A,\mathcal R)$ be a pure type system. An expression is called *legal* if it occurs as the subject or the expected type of a derivable judgment. If every legal expression of $\mathcal P$ is weakly normalizing for full $\beta$-reduction, but some legal expression is not strongly normalizing, then there are finite sets $$\mathcal S_0\subseteq\mathcal S,\qquad \mathcal A_0\subseteq\mathcal A\cap\mathcal S_0^2,\qquad \mathcal R_0\subseteq\mathcal R\cap\mathcal S_0^3$$ such that the restricted system $\mathcal P_0=(\mathcal S_0,\mathcal A_0,\mathcal R_0)$ has the same two properties.
  - Explanation: A hypothetical system-wide weakly normalizing system with a non-strongly-normalizing legal expression already has such a counterexample in a finite subspecification.
- **claim-saturated-common-context** (construction, [source](#stmt-pts-saturated-context)): Suppose that the sort set $\mathcal S$ is at most countable, and that the variable set is countably infinite. There is a possibly empty countable sequence of declarations $\Omega$ with the following properties. A judgment over $\Omega$ means a judgment derivable over some finite initial segment of $\Omega$.* 1. *Every finite initial segment of $\Omega$ is valid. An infinite set of variable names is disjoint from all its declared names.* 2. *If $P$ is a finite initial segment of $\Omega$ and $P\vdash D:s$, then $\Omega$ contains infinitely many declarations $y:D$ after $P$, with distinct declared names.* 3. *Every finite valid context embeds, by an injective renaming of its declared variables, as a subsequence of a finite initial segment of $\Omega$. If $\Gamma\vdash M:A$, such an embedding $\rho$ gives $P\vdash M\rho:A\rho$ for a finite initial segment $P$ containing the embedded context.* 4. *If $P\vdash D:s$ for a finite initial segment $P$, and $F$ is any finite set of variable names, there is a larger finite initial segment $Q$ ending in a declaration $y:D$, where $y\notin F\cup\operatorname{dom}(P)$, and $Q\vdash y:D$.* *Moreover, finitely many judgments over $\Omega$ hold over a common finite initial segment. Injective renaming in the third clause preserves and reflects weak and strong normalization.
  - Explanation: A countable saturated context accommodates finite valid contexts by injective renaming, supplies fresh variables at sorted types, and preserves normalization under those renamings.
- **claim-normal-profile-calculus** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-normal-profiles)): A sorted normal expression is a sort, a neutral expression, or a product. Its profile is determined as follows:* 1. *a literal sort $s$ has profile $\operatorname{Ax}(s)$;* 2. *a neutral expression has a singleton profile $\{s\}$, and $\operatorname{Ax}(s)$ is nonempty;* 3. *a normal product $\Pi x:D.E$ has profile $$\operatorname{Out}\bigl(\pi_\Gamma(D), \pi_{\Gamma,x:D}(E)\bigr).$$* *Profiles are unchanged by valid thinning and context conversion. If $\xi:\Gamma\to\Delta$ is a typed substitution, then $$\pi_\Gamma(T) \subseteq\pi_\Delta(T\xi). \label{eq:profile-growth}$$
  - Explanation: Normal profiles are computed from axiom targets, singleton neutral profiles and product outputs; structural context changes preserve them, while typed substitution can enlarge them.
- **claim-feasible-profile-generation** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-feasible-profiles)): The feasible profiles form the smallest family containing $$\operatorname{Ax}(s)\quad\hbox{and}\quad\{s\} \qquad\bigl(\operatorname{Ax}(s)\ne\varnothing\bigr)$$ and closed under nonempty values of $\operatorname{Out}$.
  - Explanation: The feasible profiles are exactly those generated by the stated axiom profiles and singleton profiles under nonempty product outputs.
- **claim-tested-stack-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-test-properties)): For every actual type $T$, $G_T\subseteq\top_T$. If $T$ is a literal sort, equality holds. Every variable typed at $T$ belongs to $G_T$. If $T=\Pi x:D.E$, $h\in G_T$, and $n\in\top_D$, then $h\,n\in G_{T_n}$.
  - Explanation: Passing all tested stacks implies strong normalization, includes variables, agrees with strong normalization at literal sorts, and propagates along the exact successive product types.
- **claim-sn-head-expansion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sn-head-expansion)): Suppose that $D,P,n,m_1,\ldots,m_k$ are strongly normalizing and that $$P[x:=n]m_1\cdots m_k$$ is strongly normalizing. Then $(\lambda x:D.P)n m_1\cdots m_k$ is strongly normalizing.
  - Explanation: Head expansion preserves strong normalization when the annotation, body, arguments and head contractum are strongly normalizing.
- **claim-candidate-lattice-and-product-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-candidate-lattice)): The inclusion-ordered set $\mathcal C(T)$ is a complete lattice with largest element $\top_T$. Write $\bot_T$ for its least element. For an actual product $T=\Pi x:D.E$, choose a set $A\subseteq\top_D$. For each $n\in A$ choose any family of candidates $C_{n,a}\in\mathcal C(T_n)$ indexed by a set $Q_n$. Then $$\{h\in\top_T:\text{for every }n\in A\text{ and }a\in Q_n, \ h n\in C_{n,a}\} \label{eq:product-test-candidate}$$ is a candidate at $T$.
  - Explanation: Candidates form a complete lattice, and universally testing a typed strongly normalizing function against domain terms and codomain candidates produces another candidate.
- **claim-sn-transport-fundamental** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-sn-fundamental)): Assume <a href="#eq:processed-invariant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:processed-invariant">[eq:processed-invariant]</a> and an implementation of the three interface requirements in Section <a href="#subsec:candidate-interface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:candidate-interface">3.3</a> for $S$. If $\Gamma\vdash M:A$, the expression $M$ is strongly normalizing, and $(\xi,\theta)$ is admissible, then $$M\xi\in R_{\Gamma,A^\#}(\xi,\theta). \label{eq:sn-fundamental}$$
  - Explanation: The processed invariant and a complete candidate interface transport strongly normalizing terms through admissible environments into their expected candidates.
- **claim-advance-processed-component** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-advance-component)): Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:sn-fundamental" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:sn-fundamental">19</a>, <a href="#eq:processed-invariant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:processed-invariant">[eq:processed-invariant]</a> also holds for every actual type with profile in $S$.
  - Explanation: The fundamental transport lemma extends the tested-stack invariant to the current component.
- **claim-interfaces-complete-normalization** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-interfaces-imply-sn)): If the interface can be implemented at every strongly connected component, then every legal expression of the PTS is strongly normalizing.
  - Explanation: Candidate interfaces for all components imply strong normalization by excluding a smallest nonnormalizing application.
- **claim-signed-component-interface** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-signed-component)): If $S$ has no odd closed walk, it admits the candidate interface.
  - Explanation: A component without an odd closed walk admits a sign-compatible mixed lattice order and hence a candidate fixed point supplying its interface.
- **claim-support-controls-bits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-support-dependence)): For fixed typed actual images, the candidate of an $S$ type depends on the bits of its starting context only through its support, if any. Valid thinning and context conversion preserve the support, its polarity, and the candidates. Pointwise convertible actual images preserve the candidates when old bits agree.
  - Explanation: In the single internal child construction, context bits influence a candidate only through its support; the stated structural and convertible-image changes preserve this interpretation.
- **claim-support-substitution-comparison** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-support-substitution)): Let $\Gamma,y:B,\Delta$ be valid, let $\Gamma\vdash N:B$, and let $T$ be a normal type of profile in $S$ in that context. Put $$\Gamma'=\Gamma,\Delta[y:=N],\qquad T'=(T[y:=N])^\#.$$ Take typed substitutions of the two contexts into $\Omega$ whose images are compatible with composition: the pre-substitution image of $y$ is convertible to the actual image of $N$, and the other images agree up to conversion. Equip the substitutions with bits $\theta,\theta'$. Fix a comparison sign $\delta\in\{+,-\}$ and assume:* 1. *the bits agree on any variable supported by both $T$ and $T'$;* 2. *if $y$ supports $T$ with polarity $\varepsilon$, then its pre-substitution bit is $0$ when $\delta\varepsilon=+$ and $1$ when $\delta\varepsilon=-$.* *Then, at their common actual normal type, $$R_T(\xi,\theta) \preceq_\delta R_{T'}(\xi',\theta').$$
  - Explanation: Compatible actual images and the prescribed shared-support and extremum bit conditions give the stated directional candidate comparison after generic substitution.
- **claim-one-child-component-interface** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-one-child-component)): If no profile triple has all three vertices in $S$, then the bit-parametrized candidates above implement the candidate interface.
  - Explanation: When a profile triple cannot have all its vertices in the component, the recursively defined bit-parametrized candidates satisfy the complete interface.
- **claim-secondary-sign-dependencies** (theorem, [source](#stmt-obs-graph-lemma)): An active predecessor of a plain vertex is plain. Along an edge between free vertices, the sign is preserved on a positive edge and reversed on a negative edge.* *Let $T=\Pi x:D.E$ be a normalized actual type, put $I=\pi(D)$, $J=\pi(E)$, and $K=\pi(T)$, and let $q\in\operatorname{Ty}(D)$. Put $T_q=(E[q/x])^\#$ and $L=\pi(T_q)$. If $K,L$ are active, then $J$ is active, and $I$ is active whenever $I\in H$. If instead $K,L\in S$, then $J\in S$ and every active $I$ is direct.
  - Explanation: The secondary graph controls plainness, free signs and active product dependencies; product tests remaining in the current primary component have direct active domains.
- **claim-plain-observation-spaces** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-obs-plain-construction)): Under <a href="#obs:forbidden" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="obs:forbidden">[obs:forbidden]</a>, the plain spaces admit <a href="#obs:plain-prefix" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="obs:plain-prefix">[obs:plain-prefix]</a>. They also admit distinguished values whose bases are $\mathbf t$ and whose next values are distinguished values.
  - Explanation: The graph exclusion permits plain observation spaces with the required prefix decomposition and recursively distinguished values.
- **claim-uniform-evaluation-stage-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-obs-bound-statement)): For every fixed finite tree $t$ there are bounds for each of its $\operatorname{Val}$ and $\operatorname{Read}$ evaluations, denoted below by $F_t(k)$ when the evaluation is understood, with $F_t(k)\geq k+1$, satisfying the following assertions.* 1. *Every evaluation is defined for every typed environment.* 2. *In any environment minus-bounded by $k$, a free minus $\operatorname{Val}$ output belongs to $C_{F_t(k)}$.* 3. *For two environments minus-bounded by $k$ and agreeing to level $F_t(k)$, plain and minus $\operatorname{Val}$ outputs are equal, $\operatorname{Read}$ outputs are equal, and plus $\operatorname{Val}$ outputs are $\equiv_k$-equivalent.* *The output spaces and their indices in the two environments agree: pointwise convertibility of the images gives the same normalized actual types, expression profiles, normalized argument keys, and plain type tags. Increasing a chosen bound preserves all three assertions.
  - Explanation: A fixed finite typing tree has evaluation and comparison bounds uniform in typed images and parameters. These bound semantic stages rather than normalization runtime.
- **claim-evaluation-structural-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-obs-structural-invariance)): Compatible environments give identical Val and Read outputs. Valid thinning and context conversion, with transported trees and compatible old environments, preserve both outputs. Retargeting a root to a convertible expected type preserves Val. All these operations commute with capture-avoiding renaming of generic declared and bound variables, reindexing the substitution and parameters while leaving their actual images and values unchanged.
  - Explanation: Evaluation and candidate reading respect compatible environments, transported contexts and generic renaming; retargeting a convertible root preserves its value.
- **claim-normal-syntax-goodness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-obs-goodness-basic)): Goodness has the structural invariances of Lemma <a href="#obs:structural-invariance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="obs:structural-invariance">29</a>. If the actual expression profile and $\{s\}$ are in $S$ and $\operatorname{Ax}(s)$ is active, then Read$(M:s)$ is always the base projection <a href="#obs:read-base" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="obs:read-base">[obs:read-base]</a>, at every syntactic shape, and its goodness is equivalent to Val goodness.* *Every normal syntax tree is good for active Val, under every typed raw substitution. A normal tree $M:s$ is good for Read whenever its generic expression profile belongs to $U\cup S$.
  - Explanation: Goodness is structurally invariant, agrees with the active base projection in the stated regime, and holds for normal value trees and the specified normal reading trees.
- **claim-good-value-semantic-substitution** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-obs-substitution)): Let a tree over $\Gamma,x:D,\Delta$ be given, and let $u$ be a tree of $\Gamma\vdash N:D$. Substitute $u$ capture-avoidably at occurrences of $x$, transporting the context, expectations, and generation data. Give the resulting context a typed environment, and give the original context a compatible environment whose image of $x$ is convertible to the actual expression $N\xi$. Require the remaining corresponding parameters to agree.* *Let $I=\pi((D\xi)^\#)$. Assume that $I$ is active whenever $I\in H$. If $I$ is active, assume Val$(u;\xi)$ is good and use its value as the original parameter of $x$. Then a good Val or Read evaluation before substitution is good after substitution, and the two outputs are equal.
  - Explanation: Substitution commutes with good evaluations when actual images compose compatibly, old parameters agree, and the substituted value satisfies the active-layer and goodness conditions.
- **claim-good-evaluation-reduction-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-obs-reduction-transport)): A one-step full beta reduction of a typed expression transports its tree to a tree of the reduct at the same expected type. For every environment, good Val and Read evaluations remain good and their outputs are preserved. This includes reductions in annotations.
  - Explanation: A typed full beta step transports the stored tree at the same expected type and preserves every good evaluation, including steps in annotations.
- **claim-normal-tree-independence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-obs-normal-independence)): On a fixed normal expression in a fixed generic context, Val is independent of its stored tree whenever the root expectations are convertible. Read is independent of the stored tree and of its chosen literal expected sort.
  - Explanation: On normal syntax, value evaluation is independent of stored derivations with convertible expectations, while candidate reading also ignores the chosen literal sort.
- **claim-observation-candidate-interface** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-obs-interface-theorem)): Assume system-wide weak normalization and <a href="#obs:forbidden" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="obs:forbidden">[obs:forbidden]</a>. Then <a href="#obs:R-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="obs:R-definition">[obs:R-definition]</a> gives candidates with the required weakening and context-conversion invariance and nonempty parameter extensions. It has the product test clause and the specialization property: if $T=\Pi x:D.E$ is generic normalized of profile in $S$, $\Gamma\vdash N:D$, and $N\xi$ passes its domain test, then the test of a candidate member $h$ entails $$h\,(N\xi)\in R_{(E[N/x])^\#}(\xi,\theta).$$ The ordinary typed-SN clause applies when the instantiated actual profile lies in $U$.
  - Explanation: Under weak normalization and the graph exclusion, observation reading supplies the candidate interface, including nonempty extensions, product tests and specialization at the normalized dependent codomain.
- **claim-labelled-metatheory-and-lifting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-enc-labelled-metatheory)): The labelled typing rules have context validity, correctness of types, weakening, typed substitution by terms of the declared modes, context conversion, generation, and subject reduction at the exact expected type. Labelled beta reduction is confluent, and convertible labelled products have the same pair of labels and convertible corresponding components. Erasing a labelled typing derivation gives a typing derivation in the original pure type system.* *If $\Gamma\vdash M:A$ is a labelled judgment and $\|M\|\longrightarrow_\beta N$, there is a labelled $M'$ with $$M\longrightarrow_\beta M',\qquad \|M'\|=N, \qquad \Gamma\vdash M':A,$$ up to alpha conversion. The mode of $M'$ is the mode of $M$. Consequently, every finite reduction of $\|M\|$ lifts to a typed labelled reduction. If $\|M\|$ is weakly normalizing, some labelled reduct of $M$ has beta-normal erasure.
  - Explanation: The mode-labelled calculus preserves the required metatheory, erases to ordinary typing, and lifts every erased typed beta step while retaining proof or data mode and annotations.
- **claim-finite-terminal-transformations** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-enc-terminal-converters)): There is a finite valid extension $\Delta=\Delta_{\mathsf d}, \Delta_{\mathsf p}$ and, for every pair of terminals $F,F'$, a proof term $\tau_{F,F'}(p):F'$ in $\Delta,p:F$. Every declaration of $\Delta_{\mathsf p}$ has the form $$c:\Pi^{\mathsf p,\mathsf p}u:T.F_J,$$ where $T$ is a normal, constant telescope of $(\mathsf d,\mathsf p)$ products ending at a terminal. Its suffixes are sorted, and its type contains no free proof variables.
  - Explanation: A finite ambient extension supplies proof transformations between terminals with precisely restricted converter declarations and proof-independent types.
- **claim-finite-propositional-proof-builders** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-enc-propositional)): If formulas $P_1,\ldots,P_m$ propositionally entail a formula $R$, there is a specified finite term $\mathsf{pl}_R(p_1,\ldots,p_m):R$ under $p_i:P_i$. Here $\bot$ has truth value false; specified Boolean connectives are parsed, and other subformulas, including quantifiers, may be treated as atoms.
  - Explanation: Truth-table entailment between the derived formulas yields a finite typed proof term, treating unparsed subformulas as atoms.
- **claim-path-data-cancellation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-enc-data-cancellation)): For an even data path there is a proof of $$\operatorname{use}_\rho(\operatorname{pack}_\rho(t);x.\Phi) \leftrightarrow\Phi[x:=t],$$ and for an odd data path there is a proof of $$\langle\operatorname{mk}_\rho(x.\Phi),t\rangle_\rho \leftrightarrow\Phi[x:=t].$$ These are uniform term constructors in all the displayed open data parameters. The right-hand sides contain the original input $t$.
  - Explanation: Even and odd wrapper operations have uniform logical cancellation proofs whose callbacks retain the original datum; the result grants no replacement of logically equivalent data inside functions.
- **claim-channel-path-selection** (construction, [source](#stmt-chan-paths)): There is a retained negative edge associated to a triple $(p,n,g)$, with $p,g\in C$, and there are fixed secondary paths $$\alpha:k\longrightarrow p\quad\text{odd},\qquad \beta:p\longrightarrow k\quad\text{odd},\qquad \kappa:g\longrightarrow J\quad\text{even},$$ all inside $C$. There are also fixed secondary paths $$\lambda:k\longrightarrow j\quad\text{odd},\qquad \theta:k\longrightarrow h\quad\text{even},$$ where $j$ and $h$ are direct vertices. In particular, $\eta=\beta\theta:p\longrightarrow h$ is odd.
  - Explanation: The assumed forbidden configuration supplies the retained negative edge and paths of the required parities, including paths to direct quantifier vertices.
- **claim-channel-rounding-evaluation** (construction, [source](#stmt-chan-rounding)): There are proof builders, for $y:L$ and $t:B(A)$, of $$\langle\operatorname{Round}(y),t\rangle_L \leftrightarrow\langle y,t\rangle_L. \label{chan-primitive-round}$$ For every formula $\Phi$ under $x:L$ and every $w:L$, there is a proof of $$\operatorname{pred}(x.\Phi)@w \leftrightarrow\Phi[x:=\operatorname{Round}(w)]. \label{chan-predicate-evaluation}$$
  - Explanation: Rounding preserves primitive channel observations, while a constructed predicate evaluates its callback at the rounded argument.
- **claim-double-wrapper-bit-recovery** (construction, [source](#stmt-chan-double-laws)): For $\epsilon\in\{0,1\}$ there are proofs of the signed bit $$\begin{cases} \operatorname{bit}(\operatorname{emb}_1(t)),&\epsilon=1,\\ \neg\operatorname{bit}(\operatorname{emb}_0(t)),&\epsilon=0, \end{cases}$$ and a proof of $$\operatorname{rec}(\operatorname{emb}_{\epsilon}(t);u.\Phi) \leftrightarrow\Phi[u:=t].$$
  - Explanation: A pair of odd wrappers carries a signed bit and recovers formula observations at the exact original input.
- **claim-marker-discrimination-and-decoding** (construction, [source](#stmt-chan-marker-laws)): For $P:\mathsf P_L$ and $t,u\in\mathcal T$ there are proofs of $$\operatorname{Mark}_t(\operatorname{Enc}_t(P)),\qquad \neg\operatorname{Mark}_u(\operatorname{Enc}_t(P))\quad(u\ne t).$$ For every $c:L_c$ there is a proof of $$\langle\operatorname{Dec}(\operatorname{Enc}_t(P)),c\rangle_{\mathsf P_L} \leftrightarrow\langle P,c\rangle_{\mathsf P_L},$$ and consequently, for every $y:L$, a proof of $$\operatorname{Dec}(\operatorname{Enc}_t(P))@y\leftrightarrow P@y. \label{chan-decode}$$
  - Explanation: Tagged predicate encodings satisfy their own marker, reject other markers, and decode to the original predicate observations without additional rounding.
- **claim-dummy-and-point-query-laws** (construction, [source](#stmt-chan-point-laws)): There are proofs of $$D(\operatorname{dummy}),\qquad \neg\operatorname{Mark}_t(bp_\bot)\quad(t\in\mathcal T),\qquad \neg D(\operatorname{point}(y)),$$ and there is a proof, for $P:\mathsf P_L$, of $$\operatorname{qry}_{\operatorname{pt}}(\operatorname{point}(y),P) \leftrightarrow P@y. \label{chan-point-query}$$
  - Explanation: The dummy and point objects have the required contrasting observations, and a point query returns the original predicate evaluation.
- **claim-bundle-access-exact-parameter** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-chan-access)): Under $A:s$, $m:M(A)$, $y:L(A)$ and the proof input $d_m:\neg D(m)$, there is a proof of $$\operatorname{Access}(\operatorname{Bundle}(m,y);o,x.\Psi) \leftrightarrow\Psi(m,\operatorname{Round}(y)). \label{chan-access-law}$$ The definitions of $\operatorname{Bundle}$ and $\operatorname{Access}$ themselves do not use $d_m$.
  - Explanation: Given the negative dummy observation for the parameter, access to its bundle retains that exact parameter and rounds only the channel argument. The data definitions do not depend on the proof input.
- **claim-observational-equivalence-builders** (construction, [source](#stmt-rel-equivalence)): The relation $\sim$ has reflexivity, symmetry, and transitivity builders. There is a builder $\operatorname{RoundRel}(y):y\sim\operatorname{Round}y$. For $e:a\sim a'$ and $f:c\sim c'$ there is a builder $$\operatorname{cong}(e,f): (a\sim c)\leftrightarrow(a'\sim c').$$ These builders require no admissibility assumption.
  - Explanation: The observational relation is an equivalence admitting rounding and relational congruence builders without admissibility assumptions.
- **claim-relational-predicate-congruence** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-rel-pair-congruence)): There are unrounded evaluation proofs $$c_y@w\leftrightarrow(w\sim y),\qquad u_{yz}@w\leftrightarrow((w\sim y)\lor(w\sim z)).$$ Equivalent parameters give $c_y\approx c_{y'}$ and $u_{yz}\approx u_{y'z'}$. Under $ad:\operatorname{Adm}$, the predicates $\operatorname{Is}_t,T_t$ respect $\sim$. Moreover, still under $ad$, and under $P\approx P'$, $a\sim a'$, and $z\sim z'$ there are proofs of $$\operatorname{form}(P,a,z)\leftrightarrow \operatorname{form}(P',a',z'),$$ and, under the first two of these assumptions, of $\operatorname{Pair}(P,a)\approx\operatorname{Pair}(P',a')$. In particular, under $ad$ there is $$\operatorname{pv}(P,a,z): \operatorname{Pair}(P,a)@z \leftrightarrow\operatorname{form}(P,a,z).$$
  - Explanation: Class and union evaluations are unrounded. Admissibility then makes structural queries, pairing formulas and pairing predicates respect the stated observational comparisons.
- **claim-lift-validity-and-responsive-probes** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-rel-lift-properties)): The relation $\simeq$ is an equivalence relation, with the congruence builder of Lemma <a href="#rel-equivalence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rel-equivalence">45</a>, and $$\operatorname{RoundRel}_X(x):x\simeq\operatorname{Round}_Xx.$$ Data cancellation supplies $$\operatorname{LP}(i,a): \operatorname{le}(i,\operatorname{lift}_m a)\leftrightarrow \operatorname{sb}(z_i,m,a).$$ Under $ad:\operatorname{Adm}$ there are builders $$\begin{aligned} z_i\diamond_m y&\leftrightarrow i@\operatorname{lift}_m(\operatorname{Round}y), \label{rel-probe-rounded}\\ \operatorname{LC}(e)&: \operatorname{lift}_m(a)\simeq\operatorname{lift}_m(a') \qquad(e:a\sim a'), \label{rel-lift-congruence}\\ &\operatorname{Valid}(\operatorname{lift}_m a). \label{rel-lift-valid} \end{aligned}$$ If also $ex:\operatorname{Ext}(i)$, then $$z_i\diamond_m y\leftrightarrow i@\operatorname{lift}_m y \quad\hbox{and}\quad \operatorname{Resp}(z_i). \label{rel-probe-exact}$$ If $h:\operatorname{Resp}(v)$, then $$\operatorname{Part}(v)@y\leftrightarrow v\diamond_m y. \label{rel-part-exact}$$
  - Explanation: Lifts are valid under admissibility. Probes first have rounded evaluations; extensionality gives exact evaluation and response, while responsiveness gives exact Part evaluation.
- **claim-tagged-observation-separation** (construction, [source](#stmt-rel-tags)): For $t=0,\ell,r$ there are proofs of the signed bits $H_a(x^t)$ if $b_a(t)=1$, and $\neg H_a(x^t)$ if $b_a(t)=0$, and proofs $$O_i(x^t)\leftrightarrow \operatorname{le}(i,x).$$ Each $H_a$ and $O_i$ is invariant, by biconditional, under $y\mapsto\operatorname{Round}y$. Define $$\operatorname{Ae}(y,z)= \bigwedge_{a=1,2}(H_a(y)\leftrightarrow H_a(z)) \land\bigl(\forall i.\,(O_i(y)\leftrightarrow O_i(z))\bigr).$$ Then $\operatorname{Ae}$ is an equivalence relation and there is $\operatorname{AeRound}(y): \operatorname{Ae}(y,\operatorname{Round}y)$. For each tag $t$, its restriction compares exactly $\simeq$: $$\operatorname{Ae}(x^t,x'^{\,t})\leftrightarrow x\simeq x'.$$ For distinct tags $t,u$ there is a proof of $\neg\operatorname{Ae}(x^t,x'^{\,u})$.
  - Explanation: Tagged copies carry signed observations, recover the predecessor relation, respect rounding and identify equivalent inputs within a tag while separating distinct tags.
- **claim-query-realization-and-admissibility** (construction, [source](#stmt-rel-query-realization)): For $d:i\approx i'$ there is a builder $$\operatorname{ExtC}(d): \operatorname{Ext}(i)\leftrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}(i').$$ For $e:P\approx P'$, every structural $t$ has a builder $\mathcal P_t(P)\leftrightarrow\mathcal P_t(P')$. Consequently there are proofs $$q_t(P):\operatorname{qry}_t(m_0,P)\leftrightarrow\mathcal P_t(P) \quad(t\ {\rm structural}),\qquad ad:\operatorname{Adm}\big\|_{A=A_0,m=m_0}.$$
  - Explanation: The specialized query properties respect predicate equivalence, so marker decoding realizes each structural property and supplies admissibility of the constructed query object.
- **claim-coded-observation-equivalence** (construction, [source](#stmt-rel-observation-equivalence)): There are proofs $$\operatorname{GA}(P): \operatorname{Good}(P)\leftrightarrow\mathcal P_G(P), \qquad \operatorname{SA}(y,z): (y\sim z)\leftrightarrow\operatorname{Ae}(y,z).$$ For each $t$, there are builders $$\operatorname{Tag}_t(e):x^t\sim x'^{\,t} \quad(e:x\simeq x'),\qquad \operatorname{TagBack}_t(f):x\simeq x' \quad(f:x^t\sim x'^{\,t}).$$ For $t\ne u$ there is $\operatorname{diff}_{t,u}:\neg(x^t\sim x'^{\,u})$.
  - Explanation: At the fixed specialization, good predicates coincide with the designated invariant predicates and observational equivalence coincides with the coded observations, yielding same-tag transport and distinct-tag separation.
- **claim-class-and-edge-witnesses** (construction, [source](#stmt-rel-witnesses)): For $t=0,\ell,r$ there is a proof $$\operatorname{Is}_t(y)\leftrightarrow \exists x.\,(y\sim x^t).$$ For $t=\ell,r$ there is a proof $$T_t(y,z)\leftrightarrow \exists x.\,((y\sim x^0)\land(z\sim x^t)).$$
  - Explanation: Class and edge queries at the specialization are characterized by existential witnesses in the appropriate tagged copies.
- **claim-pairing-response-and-point-laws** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-rel-pair-laws)): Under $h:\operatorname{Resp}(v)$, let $P_x=\operatorname{Pair}(\operatorname{Part}(v),x^0)$. For every $x':X$ there are proofs $$\begin{aligned} P_x@(x'^{\,\ell})&\leftrightarrow v\diamond_m(x'^{\,0}), \label{rel-pair-left}\\ P_x@(x'^{\,r})&\leftrightarrow x\simeq x'. \label{rel-pair-right} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: A responsive probe gives a pairing predicate whose left-tag observation recovers its response and whose right-tag observation identifies the paired point up to equivalence.
- **claim-response-represented-predicate** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-rel-response-predicate)): For every data parameter $v:V$, define $$i_v=\operatorname{pred}_X(x'.\,v\diamond_m(x'^{\,0})) :\mathsf P_X.$$ This data definition requires no proof hypothesis. Given $h:\operatorname{Resp}(v)$, there are builders $$E_h(x):i_v@x\leftrightarrow v\diamond_m(x^0), \qquad ex_v:\operatorname{Ext}(i_v).$$ Moreover, for $x:X$ and $u:\operatorname{Valid}(x)$, there is a builder $$\operatorname{SB}(v,h,x,u): \operatorname{sb}(v,m,x^0)\leftrightarrow \operatorname{le}(i_v,x). \label{rel-sb-law}$$
  - Explanation: The represented predicate is defined without proofs. Responsiveness supplies its exact evaluation and extensionality; a valid point additionally identifies the substitution query with its predecessor observation.
- **claim-shift-with-validity-guards** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-rel-shift)): Define the data operations $$\delta x=\operatorname{lift}_m(x^0):X, \qquad Si=\operatorname{pred}_X(x.\,i@\delta x):\mathsf P_X.$$ For every $x:X$ there is a proof $V_\delta(x):\operatorname{Valid}(\delta x)$, and for $e:x\simeq x'$ there is a proof $\operatorname{DeltaC}(e):\delta x\simeq\delta x'$. For $ex:\operatorname{Ext}(i)$ there are builders $$\begin{aligned} s_{ex}(x)&:Si@x\leftrightarrow i@\delta x, \label{rel-shift-evaluation}\\ \operatorname{ExtS}(i,ex)&:\operatorname{Ext}(Si), \label{rel-shift-extensionality}\\ d_s&:i_{z_i}\approx Si. \label{rel-shift-comparison} \end{aligned}$$ For the additional input $u:\operatorname{Valid}(x)$ there is a builder $$a_{ex,u}:\operatorname{le}(i,\delta x) \leftrightarrow\operatorname{le}(Si,x). \label{rel-shift-predecessor}$$
  - Explanation: The shift always produces a valid point and respects point equivalence. Extensionality supplies shift evaluation and predicate comparison; the predecessor equivalence additionally requires validity of the original point.
- **claim-induction-predicate-congruence** (construction, [source](#stmt-rel-ind-congruence)): For $d:i\approx i'$ there is a builder $$\operatorname{IndC}(d): \operatorname{Ind}(i)\leftrightarrow\operatorname{Ind}(i').$$ This builder needs no extensionality hypothesis on either predicate.
  - Explanation: Predicate equivalence transports the guarded induction formula without requiring either predicate to be extensional.
- **claim-valid-induction-object** (construction, [source](#stmt-rel-wf)): Define, without proof hypotheses, $$\operatorname{WF}=\operatorname{mk}_X(v.\,\operatorname{Ind}(i_v)):X.$$ There is a proof $V_{\operatorname{WF}}: \operatorname{Valid}(\operatorname{WF})$. For $ex:\operatorname{Ext}(i)$ there is a builder $$w_{ex}:\operatorname{le}(i,\operatorname{WF}) \leftrightarrow\operatorname{Ind}(Si). \label{rel-wf-evaluation}$$ Finally, from $i:\mathsf P_X$, $ex:\operatorname{Ext}(i)$ and $l:\operatorname{Ind}(i)$ there is a builder $$b(i,ex,l):i@\operatorname{WF}. \label{rel-induction-builder}$$
  - Explanation: The induction object is defined without proof hypotheses and is valid. For an extensional predicate its predecessor observation reflects shifted induction, and an induction proof yields evaluation at that object.
- **claim-guarded-diagonal-terminal-proof** (construction, [source](#stmt-rel-contradiction)): The specialized relational construction yields a proof of $\bot=F_b$ in the ambient labelled context.
  - Explanation: The specialized guarded relational diagonal constructs a terminal proof in the ambient labelled context, with temporary assumptions discharged.
- **claim-no-normal-proof-erasure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-enc-no-normal-proof)): In the ambient context above, or in any legal extension by data declarations, there is no proof-mode term $P$ with beta-normal erasure and a judgment $P:T$ for any $T\in\mathcal T$.
  - Explanation: The restricted converter context and its legal data extensions admit no proof-mode inhabitant of a terminal or converter suffix with normal erasure.
- **claim-weak-normalization-excludes-configuration** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-enc-exclusion)): Assume that every legal expression of the given pure type system is weakly beta-normalizing. For every primary component containing an odd closed walk and an all-component profile triple, condition <a href="#obs:forbidden" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="obs:forbidden">[obs:forbidden]</a> holds.
  - Explanation: System-wide weak normalization rules out the forbidden configuration: the constructed terminal proof would normalize after erasure, lift back with proof mode intact, and contradict the normal-erasure obstruction.

        "exact_quote": "For derivable judgments in an arbitrary pure type system:*\n\n1.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash x:T$, then $x:D$ occurs in $\\Gamma$ and $T=_\\beta D$.*\n\n2.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash s:T$, then $(s,u)\\in\\mathcal A$ and $T=_\\beta u$ for some sort $u$.*\n\n3.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash\\Pi x:D.E:T$, then for some $(a,b,c)\\in\\mathcal R$, $$\\Gamma\\vdash D:a,\\qquad\n           \\Gamma,x:D\\vdash E:b,\\qquad T=_\\beta c.$$*\n\n4.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash\\lambda x:D.M:T$, then for some $E,c$, $$\\Gamma,x:D\\vdash M:E,\\qquad\n           \\Gamma\\vdash\\Pi x:D.E:c,\\qquad\n           T=_\\beta\\Pi x:D.E.$$*\n\n5.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash MN:T$, then for some $D,E,x$, $$\\Gamma\\vdash M:\\Pi x:D.E,\\qquad\n           \\Gamma\\vdash N:D,\\qquad T=_\\beta E[x:=N].$$*\n\n*Every immediate syntactic child of a typable expression is typable in the corresponding context, with a binder declaration added for a body or codomain.",

        {

          "exact_quote": "For derivable judgments in an arbitrary pure type system:*\n\n1.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash x:T$, then $x:D$ occurs in $\\Gamma$ and $T=_\\beta D$.*\n\n2.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash s:T$, then $(s,u)\\in\\mathcal A$ and $T=_\\beta u$ for some sort $u$.*\n\n3.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash\\Pi x:D.E:T$, then for some $(a,b,c)\\in\\mathcal R$, $$\\Gamma\\vdash D:a,\\qquad\n           \\Gamma,x:D\\vdash E:b,\\qquad T=_\\beta c.$$*\n\n4.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash\\lambda x:D.M:T$, then for some $E,c$, $$\\Gamma,x:D\\vdash M:E,\\qquad\n           \\Gamma\\vdash\\Pi x:D.E:c,\\qquad\n           T=_\\beta\\Pi x:D.E.$$*\n\n5.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash MN:T$, then for some $D,E,x$, $$\\Gamma\\vdash M:\\Pi x:D.E,\\qquad\n           \\Gamma\\vdash N:D,\\qquad T=_\\beta E[x:=N].$$*\n\n*Every immediate syntactic child of a typable expression is typable in the corresponding context, with a binder declaration added for a body or codomain.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For derivable judgments in an arbitrary pure type system:*\n\n1.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash x:T$, then $x:D$ occurs in $\\Gamma$ and $T=_\\beta D$.*\n\n2.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash s:T$, then $(s,u)\\in\\mathcal A$ and $T=_\\beta u$ for some sort $u$.*\n\n3.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash\\Pi x:D.E:T$, then for some $(a,b,c)\\in\\mathcal R$, $$\\Gamma\\vdash D:a,\\qquad\n           \\Gamma,x:D\\vdash E:b,\\qquad T=_\\beta c.$$*\n\n4.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash\\lambda x:D.M:T$, then for some $E,c$, $$\\Gamma,x:D\\vdash M:E,\\qquad\n           \\Gamma\\vdash\\Pi x:D.E:c,\\qquad\n           T=_\\beta\\Pi x:D.E.$$*\n\n5.  *If $\\Gamma\\vdash MN:T$, then for some $D,E,x$, $$\\Gamma\\vdash M:\\Pi x:D.E,\\qquad\n           \\Gamma\\vdash N:D,\\qquad T=_\\beta E[x:=N].$$*\n\n*Every immediate syntactic child of a typable expression is typable in the corresponding context, with a binder declaration added for a body or codomain.",

        "anchor": "stmt-pts-finite-restriction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal P=(\\mathcal S,\\mathcal A,\\mathcal R)$ be a pure type system. An expression is called *legal* if it occurs as the subject or the expected type of a derivable judgment.         "source_id": "PM-245.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pts-saturated-context",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that the sort set $\\mathcal S$ is at most countable, and that the variable set is countably infinite. There is a possibly empty countable sequence of declarations $\\Omega$ with the following properties. A judgment over $\\Omega$ means a judgment derivable over some finite initial segment of $\\Omega$.*\n\n1.  *Every finite initial segment of $\\Omega$ is valid. An infinite set of variable names is disjoint from all its declared names.*\n\n2.  *If $P$ is a finite initial segment of $\\Omega$ and $P\\vdash D:s$, then $\\Omega$ contains infinitely many declarations $y:D$ after $P$, with distinct declared names.*\n\n3.  *Every finite valid context embeds, by an injective renaming of its declared variables, as a subsequence of a finite initial segment of $\\Omega$. If $\\Gamma\\vdash M:A$, such an embedding $\\rho$ gives $P\\vdash M\\rho:A\\rho$ for a finite initial segment $P$ containing the embedded context.*\n\n4.  *If $P\\vdash D:s$ for a finite initial segment $P$, and $F$ is any finite set of variable names, there is a larger finite initial segment $Q$ ending in a declaration $y:D$, where $y\\notin F\\cup\\operatorname{dom}(P)$, and $Q\\vdash y:D$.*\n\n*Moreover, finitely many judgments over $\\Omega$ hold over a common finite initial segment. Injective renaming in the third clause preserves and reflects weak and strong normalization.",
        "source_id": "PM-245.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-normal-profiles",
        "exact_quote": "A sorted normal expression is a sort, a neutral expression, or a product. Its profile is determined as follows:*\n\n1.  *a literal sort $s$ has profile $\\operatorname{Ax}(s)$;*\n\n2.  *a neutral expression has a singleton profile $\\{s\\}$, and $\\operatorname{Ax}(s)$ is nonempty;*\n\n3.  *a normal product $\\Pi x:D.E$ has profile $$\\operatorname{Out}\\bigl(\\pi_\\Gamma(D),\n                             \\pi_{\\Gamma,x:D}(E)\\bigr).$$*\n\n*Profiles are unchanged by valid thinning and context conversion. If $\\xi:\\Gamma\\to\\Delta$ is a typed substitution, then $$\\pi_\\Gamma(T)\n                    \\subseteq\\pi_\\Delta(T\\xi).\n\\label{eq:profile-growth}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-245.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-normal-profiles",
          "exact_quote": "A sorted normal expression is a sort, a neutral expression, or a product. Its profile is determined as follows:*\n\n1.  *a literal sort $s$ has profile $\\operatorname{Ax}(s)$;*\n\n2.  *a neutral expression has a singleton profile $\\{s\\}$, and $\\operatorname{Ax}(s)$ is nonempty;*\n\n3.  *a normal product $\\Pi x:D.E$ has profile $$\\operatorname{Out}\\bigl(\\pi_\\Gamma(D),\n                             \\pi_{\\Gamma,x:D}(E)\\bigr).$$*\n\n*Profiles are unchanged by valid thinning and context conversion. If $\\xi:\\Gamma\\to\\Delta$ is a typed substitution, then $$\\pi_\\Gamma(T)\n                    \\subseteq\\pi_\\Delta(T\\xi).\n\\label{eq:profile-growth}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "A sorted normal expression is a sort, a neutral expression, or a product. If $\\xi:\\Gamma\\to\\Delta$ is a typed substitution, then $$\\pi_\\Gamma(T)\n                    \\subseteq\\pi_\\Delta(T\\xi).\n\\label{eq:profile-growth}$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-candidate-lattice",
        "exact_quote": "The inclusion-ordered set $\\mathcal C(T)$ is a complete lattice with largest element $\\top_T$. Write $\\bot_T$ for its least element. Then $$\\{h\\in\\top_T:\\text{for every }n\\in A\\text{ and }a\\in Q_n,\n                                  \\ h n\\in C_{n,a}\\}\n\\label{eq:product-test-candidate}$$ is a candidate at $T$.",
        "source_id": "PM-245.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-support-substitution",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma,y:B,\\Delta$ be valid, let $\\Gamma\\vdash N:B$, and let $T$ be a normal type of profile in $S$ in that context. Put $$\\Gamma'=\\Gamma,\\Delta[y:=N],\\qquad\n                         T'=(T[y:=N])^\\#.$$ Take typed substitutions of the two contexts into $\\Omega$ whose images are compatible with composition: the pre-substitution image of $y$ is convertible to the actual image of $N$, and the other images agree up to conversion. Equip the substitutions with bits $\\theta,\\theta'$. Fix a comparison sign $\\delta\\in\\{+,-\\}$ and assume:*\n\n1.  *the bits agree on any variable supported by both $T$ and $T'$;*\n\n2.  *if $y$ supports $T$ with polarity $\\varepsilon$, then its pre-substitution bit is $0$ when $\\delta\\varepsilon=+$ and $1$ when $\\delta\\varepsilon=-$.*\n\n*Then, at their common actual normal type, $$R_T(\\xi,\\theta)\n                  \\preceq_\\delta R_{T'}(\\xi',\\theta').$$",
        "source_id": "PM-245.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-support-substitution",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma,y:B,\\Delta$ be valid, let $\\Gamma\\vdash N:B$, and let $T$ be a normal type of profile in $S$ in that context. Equip the substitutions with bits $\\theta,\\theta'$. Fix a comparison sign $\\delta\\in\\{+,-\\}$ and assume:*\n\n1.  *the bits agree on any variable supported by both $T$ and $T'$;*\n\n2.  *if $y$ supports $T$ with polarity $\\varepsilon$, then its pre-substitution bit is $0$ when $\\delta\\varepsilon=+$ and $1$ when $\\delta\\varepsilon=-$.*\n\n*Then, at their common actual normal type, $$R_T(\\xi,\\theta)\n                  \\preceq_\\delta R_{T'}(\\xi',\\theta').$$",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-obs-graph-lemma",
        "exact_quote": "An active predecessor of a plain vertex is plain.         "anchor": "stmt-obs-bound-statement",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed finite tree $t$ there are bounds for each of its $\\operatorname{Val}$ and $\\operatorname{Read}$ evaluations, denoted below by $F_t(k)$ when the evaluation is understood, with $F_t(k)\\geq k+1$, satisfying the following assertions.*\n\n1.  *Every evaluation is defined for every typed environment.*\n\n2.  *In any environment minus-bounded by $k$, a free minus $\\operatorname{Val}$ output belongs to $C_{F_t(k)}$.*\n\n3.  *For two environments minus-bounded by $k$ and agreeing to level $F_t(k)$, plain and minus $\\operatorname{Val}$ outputs are equal, $\\operatorname{Read}$ outputs are equal, and plus $\\operatorname{Val}$ outputs are $\\equiv_k$-equivalent.*\n\n*The output spaces and their indices in the two environments agree: pointwise convertibility of the images gives the same normalized actual types, expression profiles, normalized argument keys, and plain type tags.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-obs-structural-invariance",
        "exact_quote": "Compatible environments give identical Val and Read outputs. Valid thinning and context conversion, with transported trees and compatible old environments, preserve both outputs. Retargeting a root to a convertible expected type preserves Val.         "anchor": "stmt-obs-substitution",
        "exact_quote": "Let a tree over $\\Gamma,x:D,\\Delta$ be given, and let $u$ be a tree of $\\Gamma\\vdash N:D$. Substitute $u$ capture-avoidably at occurrences of $x$, transporting the context, expectations, and generation data. Give the resulting context a typed environment, and give the original context a compatible environment whose image of $x$ is convertible to the actual expression $N\\xi$. Require the remaining corresponding parameters to agree.*\n\n*Let $I=\\pi((D\\xi)^\\#)$. Assume that $I$ is active whenever $I\\in H$. If $I$ is active, assume Val$(u;\\xi)$ is good and use its value as the original parameter of $x$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-enc-labelled-metatheory",
        "exact_quote": "The labelled typing rules have context validity, correctness of types, weakening, typed substitution by terms of the declared modes, context conversion, generation, and subject reduction at the exact expected type. Labelled beta reduction is confluent, and convertible labelled products have the same pair of labels and convertible corresponding components. Erasing a labelled typing derivation gives a typing derivation in the original pure type system.*\n\n*If $\\Gamma\\vdash M:A$ is a labelled judgment and $|M|\\longrightarrow_\\beta N$, there is a labelled $M'$ with $$M\\longrightarrow_\\beta M',\\qquad |M'|=N,\n \\qquad \\Gamma\\vdash M':A,$$ up to alpha conversion. The mode of $M'$ is the mode of $M$. Consequently, every finite reduction of $|M|$ lifts to a typed labelled reduction. If $|M|$ is weakly normalizing, some labelled reduct of $M$ has beta-normal erasure.",
        "source_id": "PM-245.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-enc-labelled-metatheory",

Erasing a labelled typing derivation gives a typing derivation in the original pure type system.*\n\n*If $\\Gamma\\vdash M:A$ is a labelled judgment and $|M|\\longrightarrow_\\beta N$, there is a labelled $M'$ with $$M\\longrightarrow_\\beta M',\\qquad |M'|=N,\n \\qquad \\Gamma\\vdash M':A,$$ up to alpha conversion. The mode of $M'$ is the mode of $M$. Consequently, every finite reduction of $|M|$ lifts to a typed labelled reduction. If $|M|$ is weakly normalizing, some labelled reduct of $M$ has beta-normal erasure.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        }
      ],

        "exact_quote": "There is a finite valid extension $\\Delta=\\Delta_{\\mathsf d},\n\\Delta_{\\mathsf p}$ and, for every pair of terminals $F,F'$, a proof term $\\tau_{F,F'}(p):F'$ in $\\Delta,p:F$.         "exact_quote": "There are unrounded evaluation proofs $$c_y@w\\leftrightarrow(w\\sim y),\\qquad\n u_{yz}@w\\leftrightarrow((w\\sim y)\\lor(w\\sim z)).$$ Equivalent parameters give $c_y\\approx c_{y'}$ and $u_{yz}\\approx u_{y'z'}$. Under $ad:\\operatorname{Adm}$, the predicates $\\operatorname{Is}_t,T_t$ respect $\\sim$. Moreover, still under $ad$, and under $P\\approx P'$, $a\\sim a'$, and $z\\sim z'$ there are proofs of $$\\operatorname{form}(P,a,z)\\leftrightarrow\n       \\operatorname{form}(P',a',z'),$$ and, under the first two of these assumptions, of $\\operatorname{Pair}(P,a)\\approx\\operatorname{Pair}(P',a')$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rel-lift-properties",
        "exact_quote": "The relation $\\simeq$ is an equivalence relation, with the congruence builder of Lemma <a href=\"#rel-equivalence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rel-equivalence\">45</a>, and $$\\operatorname{RoundRel}_X(x):x\\simeq\\operatorname{Round}_Xx.$$ Data cancellation supplies $$\\operatorname{LP}(i,a):\n       \\operatorname{le}(i,\\operatorname{lift}_m a)\\leftrightarrow \\operatorname{sb}(z_i,m,a).$$ Under $ad:\\operatorname{Adm}$ there are builders $$\\begin{aligned}\n z_i\\diamond_m y&\\leftrightarrow\n             i@\\operatorname{lift}_m(\\operatorname{Round}y),\n                  \\label{rel-probe-rounded}\\\\\n \\operatorname{LC}(e)&:\n       \\operatorname{lift}_m(a)\\simeq\\operatorname{lift}_m(a')\n                    \\qquad(e:a\\sim a'), \\label{rel-lift-congruence}\\\\\n &\\operatorname{Valid}(\\operatorname{lift}_m a). \\label{rel-lift-valid}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If also $ex:\\operatorname{Ext}(i)$, then $$z_i\\diamond_m y\\leftrightarrow i@\\operatorname{lift}_m y\n \\quad\\hbox{and}\\quad \\operatorname{Resp}(z_i).\n \\label{rel-probe-exact}$$ If $h:\\operatorname{Resp}(v)$, then $$\\operatorname{Part}(v)@y\\leftrightarrow v\\diamond_m y.\n \\label{rel-part-exact}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-245.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rel-lift-properties",

        "anchor": "stmt-rel-tags",
        "exact_quote": "For $t=0,\\ell,r$ there are proofs of the signed bits $H_a(x^t)$ if $b_a(t)=1$, and $\\neg H_a(x^t)$ if $b_a(t)=0$, and proofs $$O_i(x^t)\\leftrightarrow \\operatorname{le}(i,x).$$ Each $H_a$ and $O_i$ is invariant, by biconditional, under $y\\mapsto\\operatorname{Round}y$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Pure type specification** ([source](#stmt-pts-specification)): A *pure type specification* is a triple $\mathcal P=(\mathcal S,\mathcal A,\mathcal R)$, where $\mathcal S$ is a set of *sorts*, $\mathcal A\subseteq\mathcal S^2$ is a relation of *axioms*, and $\mathcal R\subseteq\mathcal S^3$ is a relation of *product rules*.
- **Full beta reduction** ([source](#stmt-pts-beta)): The relation $\to_\beta$ is the compatible closure of $$(\lambda x:A.M)N\to_\beta M[x:=N].$$
- **Weak and strong expression normalization** ([source](#stmt-pts-beta)): An expression is *weakly normalizing* if it reduces to a normal expression, and *strongly normalizing* if it starts no infinite reduction sequence.
- **Valid context** ([source](#stmt-pts-normalization)): A context $(x_1:A_1,\ldots,x_n:A_n)$ is *valid* if each $A_i$ is typed at some literal sort in the preceding prefix.
- **Legal expression** ([source](#stmt-pts-normalization)): It is *legal* in $\Gamma$ if it is the subject or the expected type of a derivable judgment in $\Gamma$.
- **System-wide normalization** ([source](#stmt-pts-normalization)): The system is weakly normalizing, respectively strongly normalizing, if every legal expression in every context is weakly normalizing, respectively strongly normalizing.
- **Neutral expression** ([source](#pts:neutrals)): An expression $xN_1\cdots N_k$, with $k\geq0$, is called *neutral*.
- **Sort profile** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For an expected type $T$ in a valid context $\Gamma$, define its *profile* by $$\pi_\Gamma(T)=\{s:\Gamma\vdash T^\#:s\}.$$
- **Actual type** ([source](#pm-h-13)): An *actual type* is a normal sorted expression over $\Omega$, or a literal sort.
- **Tested stacks and processed invariant** ([source](#eq:processed-invariant)): A *tested stack at $T$* is a finite list defined inductively. The empty list is always a tested stack. If $T=\Pi x:D.E$, $n\in\top_D$, and $\vec m$ is a tested stack at $$T_n=(E[x:=n])^\#,$$ then $(n,\vec m)$ is a tested stack at $T$. This definition follows these exact successive types; it does not range over alternative typings of a term. Define $$G_T=\{h\in\operatorname{Ty}(T): h\,\vec n\in\operatorname{SN}_\beta \text{ for every tested stack }\vec n\text{ at }T\}.$$ We shall establish, component by component, the invariant $$G_T=\top_T\qquad(\pi(T)\in U). \label{eq:processed-invariant}$$
- **Candidate at an actual type** ([source](#stmt-def-candidate)): A candidate at $T$ is a set $C$ satisfying $G_T\subseteq C\subseteq\top_T$ and the following head-expansion property: if $$r=(\lambda x:D.P)n\,\vec m\in\operatorname{Ty}(T),$$ all displayed components $D,P,n,\vec m$ are strongly normalizing, and $P[x:=n]\vec m\in C$, then $r\in C$.
- **Logical and raw channel values** ([source](#stmt-chan-raw-log)): For $y:L$, $c:L_c$, and $P:\mathsf P_L$, put $$\begin{aligned} \operatorname{Raw}(y) &=\operatorname{mk}_{L_c}(t.\langle y,t\rangle_L):L_c,\\ \operatorname{Log}(c) &=\operatorname{mk}_{L}(t.\langle c,t\rangle_{L_c}):L,\\ \operatorname{Round}(y)&=\operatorname{Log}(\operatorname{Raw}(y)):L,\\ P@y&=\langle P,\operatorname{Raw}(y)\rangle_{\mathsf P_L}. \end{aligned}$$ For a formula $\Phi$ under $x:L$, define $$\operatorname{pred}(x.\Phi) =\operatorname{mk}_{\mathsf P_L} (c.\Phi[x:=\operatorname{Log}(c)]):\mathsf P_L.$$ Here $t:B(A)$ and $c:L_c(A)$ in their respective callbacks.
- **Double wrapper** ([source](#stmt-chan-double)): Let $T_1=W_{\rho}(T)$ and $T_2=W_{\sigma}(T_1)$ for two odd data paths. Use subscripts $1$ and $2$ for their respective constructors and evaluations. For $t:T$ define two data embeddings $$\operatorname{emb}_1(t) =\operatorname{mk}_2(f.\langle f,t\rangle_1),\qquad \operatorname{emb}_0(t) =\operatorname{mk}_2(f.\neg\langle f,t\rangle_1), \qquad f:T_1.$$ Both have type $T_2$. Set $$\operatorname{if}(G;H,H')=(G\land H)\lor(\neg G\land H'),$$ and, for $v:T_2$ and a formula $\Phi$ under $u:T$, set $$\begin{aligned} \operatorname{bit}(v) &=\langle v,\operatorname{mk}_1(u.\top)\rangle_2,\\ \operatorname{rec}(v;u.\Phi) &=\operatorname{if}\bigl(\operatorname{bit}(v); \langle v,\operatorname{mk}_1(u.\Phi)\rangle_2, \neg\langle v,\operatorname{mk}_1(u.\Phi)\rangle_2\bigr). \end{aligned}$$
- **Bundle and access** ([source](#stmt-chan-bundle-definition)): For $m:M(A)$ and $y:L(A)$, put $$\operatorname{Bundle}(m,y)=\operatorname{mk}_{\operatorname{Single}} \left(f.\operatorname{if}\bigl( \langle f,\operatorname{dummy}\rangle_F; \langle f,m\rangle_F, \langle f,\operatorname{point}(y)\rangle_F\bigr)\right) :\operatorname{Single}(A).$$ Let $\Psi(o,x)$ be a formula under $o:M(A),x:L(A)$, independent of the new variable $w:\operatorname{Single}(A)$. Its other free data parameters are allowed and remain fixed. Define $$\begin{aligned} f_i(o)&=\operatorname{mk}_{F}\left(o'. \operatorname{if}\bigl(D(o');\bot, \operatorname{qry}_{\operatorname{pt}} (o',\operatorname{pred}(x.\Psi(o,x)))\bigr)\right),\\ f_e(w)&=\operatorname{mk}_{F}\left(o. \operatorname{if}\bigl(D(o);\top, \langle w,f_i(o)\rangle_{\operatorname{Single}}\bigr)\right),\\ \operatorname{Access}(w;o,x.\Psi) &=\langle w,f_e(w)\rangle_{\operatorname{Single}}. \end{aligned} \label{chan-access-definition}$$ Here $o,o':M(A)$, both $f_i(o)$ and $f_e(w)$ have type $F(A)$, and $\operatorname{Access}$ is a formula. The dependence of $f_i$ and $f_e$ on $\Psi$ is suppressed only in their names.
- **Admissible queries and observational equivalence** ([source](#stmt-rel-admissible)): Define $$\begin{aligned} P\approx P'&=\forall y.\,(P@y\leftrightarrow P'@y),\\ i\approx i'&=\forall x.\,(i@x\leftrightarrow i'@x),\\ \operatorname{Adm} &=\neg D(m)\land \bigwedge_{t\ {\rm structural}}\forall P,P'.\, \bigl(P\approx P'\Rightarrow (\operatorname{qry}_t(m,P)\leftrightarrow \operatorname{qry}_t(m,P'))\bigr),\\ \operatorname{Good}(P) &=\bigl(\forall y.\,(P@y\leftrightarrow P@\operatorname{Round}y)\bigr) \land\operatorname{qry}_G(m,P),\\ y\sim z&=\forall P.\, \bigl(\operatorname{Good}(P)\Rightarrow (P@y\leftrightarrow P@z)\bigr). \end{aligned}$$ The finite Boolean operations have fixed bracketing. From $ad:\operatorname{Adm}$ obtain $d_m:\neg D(m)$ by projection. For each structural $t$, projection followed by two instantiations and application defines $$\operatorname{qr}_t(e): \operatorname{qry}_t(m,P)\leftrightarrow \operatorname{qry}_t(m,P') \qquad(e:P\approx P').$$
- **Class, union and pairing predicates** ([source](#stmt-rel-pair-definition)): Put $$\begin{aligned} c_y&=\operatorname{pred}(w.w\sim y),\\ u_{yz}&=\operatorname{pred}(w.(w\sim y)\lor(w\sim z)),\\ \operatorname{Is}_t(y)&=\operatorname{qry}_{C_t}(m,c_y) &&(t=0,\ell,r),\\ T_t(y,z)&=\operatorname{Is}_0(y)\land \operatorname{Is}_t(z)\land \operatorname{qry}_{E_t}(m,u_{yz}) &&(t=\ell,r),\\ \operatorname{form}(P,a,z) &=\bigl(\exists y.\,(T_\ell(y,z)\land P@y)\bigr) \lor T_r(a,z),\\ \operatorname{Pair}(P,a)&= \operatorname{pred}(z.\operatorname{form}(P,a,z)). \end{aligned}$$ These are data definitions, independent of any proof of $\operatorname{Adm}$.
- **Lifts and uniform probes** ([source](#stmt-rel-lifts)): Define data, still with open $A,m$, by $$\begin{aligned} \operatorname{Part}(v)&=\operatorname{pred}(y.v\diamond_m y),\\ \operatorname{sb}(v,m,a)&=\operatorname{qry}_R (m,\operatorname{Pair}(\operatorname{Part}(v),a)),\\ \operatorname{lift}_m(a)&=\operatorname{mk}_X(v.\operatorname{sb}(v,m,a)). \end{aligned}$$ For $i:\mathsf P_X$, define $z_i:V$ by the following data lambda. At $z:Z$, pack along the even path $g\to J$ the data function $$\lambda^{\mathsf d,\mathsf d}w: \operatorname{Single}(z^\downarrow). \operatorname{send}_n \bigl(\operatorname{Access} (w;o,y.i@\operatorname{lift}_o(y))\bigr),$$ where all channel types and the lift in its body use $A=z^\downarrow$. This defines the body of $\lambda^{\mathsf d,\mathsf d}z:Z.\,\cdots$. The variable $o$ is the data parameter supplied by $\operatorname{Access}$. Thus $z_i$ has no free $A,m$. Put $$\begin{aligned} \operatorname{le}(i,x)&=[x z_i],\\ x\simeq x'&=\forall i.\,(\operatorname{le}(i,x)\leftrightarrow \operatorname{le}(i,x')),\\ \operatorname{Valid}(x)&=\forall i,i'.\, \bigl(i\approx i'\Rightarrow (\operatorname{le}(i,x)\leftrightarrow \operatorname{le}(i',x))\bigr),\\ \operatorname{Ext}(i)&=\forall x,x'.\, \bigl(x\simeq x'\Rightarrow(i@x\leftrightarrow i@x')\bigr),\\ \operatorname{Resp}(v)&=\forall y,z.\, \bigl(y\sim z\Rightarrow (v\diamond_m y\leftrightarrow v\diamond_m z)\bigr). \end{aligned}$$
- **Specialized query object** ([source](#stmt-rel-specialized-object)): At $A_0$, for $P:\mathsf P_L$, define $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal P_G(P)&=\forall y,z.\, \bigl(\operatorname{Ae}(y,z)\Rightarrow(P@y\leftrightarrow P@z)\bigr),\\ \mathcal P_{C_t}(P)&=\exists x.\forall w.\, (P@w\leftrightarrow\operatorname{Ae}(w,x^t)),\\ \mathcal P_{E_t}(P)&=\exists x.\forall w.\, \bigl(P@w\leftrightarrow (\operatorname{Ae}(w,x^0)\lor\operatorname{Ae}(w,x^t))\bigr),\\ i_P&=\operatorname{pred}_X(x.P@(x^\ell)),\\ \mathcal P_R(P)&=\operatorname{Ext}(i_P)\Rightarrow \forall x.\bigl(\operatorname{Valid}(x)\Rightarrow (P@(x^r)\Rightarrow \operatorname{le}(i_P,x))\bigr). \end{aligned}$$ Here $C_t$ has $t=0,\ell,r$ and $E_t$ has $t=\ell,r$. Define $$m_0=\operatorname{mk}_M \left(bp.\bigvee_{t\ {\rm structural}} \bigl(\operatorname{Mark}_t(bp)\land \mathcal P_t(\operatorname{Dec}(bp))\bigr)\right).$$ The formulas $\mathcal P_t$ use $z_i$, tags, and observations already defined independently of $m_0$; this is a data term with no recursive occurrence of itself.

Set $$\\operatorname{if}(G;H,H')=(G\\land H)\\lor(\\neg G\\land H'),$$ and, for $v:T_2$ and a formula $\\Phi$ under $u:T$, set $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\operatorname{bit}(v)\n   &=\\langle v,\\operatorname{mk}_1(u.\\top)\\rangle_2,\\\\\n \\operatorname{rec}(v;u.\\Phi)\n   &=\\operatorname{if}\\bigl(\\operatorname{bit}(v);\n          \\langle v,\\operatorname{mk}_1(u.\\Phi)\\rangle_2,\n          \\neg\\langle v,\\operatorname{mk}_1(u.\\Phi)\\rangle_2\\bigr).\n\\end{aligned}$$",

      "interpretation": "The bundle combines a data parameter with a channel value; access selects the exact parameter and evaluates a callback using the rounded channel value.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rel-admissible",
          "exact_quote": "Define $$\\begin{aligned}\n P\\approx P'&=\\forall y.\\,(P@y\\leftrightarrow P'@y),\\\\\n i\\approx i'&=\\forall x.\\,(i@x\\leftrightarrow i'@x),\\\\\n \\operatorname{Adm}\n   &=\\neg D(m)\\land\n       \\bigwedge_{t\\ {\\rm structural}}\\forall P,P'.\\,\n       \\bigl(P\\approx P'\\Rightarrow\n          (\\operatorname{qry}_t(m,P)\\leftrightarrow\n           \\operatorname{qry}_t(m,P'))\\bigr),\\\\\n \\operatorname{Good}(P)\n   &=\\bigl(\\forall y.\\,(P@y\\leftrightarrow\n                     P@\\operatorname{Round}y)\\bigr)\n           \\land\\operatorname{qry}_G(m,P),\\\\\n y\\sim z&=\\forall P.\\,\n       \\bigl(\\operatorname{Good}(P)\\Rightarrow\n                      (P@y\\leftrightarrow P@z)\\bigr).\n\\end{aligned}$$ The finite Boolean operations have fixed bracketing. From $ad:\\operatorname{Adm}$ obtain $d_m:\\neg D(m)$ by projection. For each structural $t$, projection followed by two instantiations and application defines $$\\operatorname{qr}_t(e):\n \\operatorname{qry}_t(m,P)\\leftrightarrow\n \\operatorname{qry}_t(m,P')\n \\qquad(e:P\\approx P').$$",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "Define $$\\begin{aligned}\n P\\approx P'&=\\forall y.\\,(P@y\\leftrightarrow P'@y),\\\\\n i\\approx i'&=\\forall x.\\,(i@x\\leftrightarrow i'@x),\\\\\n \\operatorname{Adm}\n   &=\\neg D(m)\\land\n       \\bigwedge_{t\\ {\\rm structural}}\\forall P,P'.\\,\n       \\bigl(P\\approx P'\\Rightarrow\n          (\\operatorname{qry}_t(m,P)\\leftrightarrow\n           \\operatorname{qry}_t(m,P'))\\bigr),\\\\\n \\operatorname{Good}(P)\n   &=\\bigl(\\forall y.\\,(P@y\\leftrightarrow\n                     P@\\operatorname{Round}y)\\bigr)\n           \\land\\operatorname{qry}_G(m,P),\\\\\n y\\sim z&=\\forall P.\\,\n       \\bigl(\\operatorname{Good}(P)\\Rightarrow\n                      (P@y\\leftrightarrow P@z)\\bigr).\n\\end{aligned}$$ The finite Boolean operations have fixed bracketing. From $ad:\\operatorname{Adm}$ obtain $d_m:\\neg D(m)$ by projection.       "interpretation": "Admissibility constrains structural query respect, while good predicates determine observational equivalence and explicitly respect rounding.",

          "anchor": "stmt-rel-pair-definition",
          "exact_quote": "Put $$\\begin{aligned}\n c_y&=\\operatorname{pred}(w.w\\sim y),\\\\\n u_{yz}&=\\operatorname{pred}(w.(w\\sim y)\\lor(w\\sim z)),\\\\\n \\operatorname{Is}_t(y)&=\\operatorname{qry}_{C_t}(m,c_y)\n                         &&(t=0,\\ell,r),\\\\\n T_t(y,z)&=\\operatorname{Is}_0(y)\\land\n             \\operatorname{Is}_t(z)\\land\n             \\operatorname{qry}_{E_t}(m,u_{yz})\n                         &&(t=\\ell,r),\\\\\n \\operatorname{form}(P,a,z)\n       &=\\bigl(\\exists y.\\,(T_\\ell(y,z)\\land P@y)\\bigr)\n                         \\lor T_r(a,z),\\\\\n \\operatorname{Pair}(P,a)&=\n             \\operatorname{pred}(z.\\operatorname{form}(P,a,z)).\n\\end{aligned}$$ These are data definitions, independent of any proof of $\\operatorname{Adm}$.",

      "exact_definition": "Put $$\\begin{aligned}\n c_y&=\\operatorname{pred}(w.w\\sim y),\\\\\n u_{yz}&=\\operatorname{pred}(w.(w\\sim y)\\lor(w\\sim z)),\\\\\n \\operatorname{Is}_t(y)&=\\operatorname{qry}_{C_t}(m,c_y)\n                         &&(t=0,\\ell,r),\\\\\n T_t(y,z)&=\\operatorname{Is}_0(y)\\land\n             \\operatorname{Is}_t(z)\\land\n             \\operatorname{qry}_{E_t}(m,u_{yz})\n                         &&(t=\\ell,r),\\\\\n \\operatorname{form}(P,a,z)\n       &=\\bigl(\\exists y.\\,(T_\\ell(y,z)\\land P@y)\\bigr)\n                         \\lor T_r(a,z),\\\\\n \\operatorname{Pair}(P,a)&=\n             \\operatorname{pred}(z.\\operatorname{form}(P,a,z)).\n\\end{aligned}$$ These are data definitions, independent of any proof of $\\operatorname{Adm}$.",

          "exact_quote": "Define data, still with open $A,m$, by $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\operatorname{Part}(v)&=\\operatorname{pred}(y.v\\diamond_m y),\\\\\n \\operatorname{sb}(v,m,a)&=\\operatorname{qry}_R\n                    (m,\\operatorname{Pair}(\\operatorname{Part}(v),a)),\\\\\n \\operatorname{lift}_m(a)&=\\operatorname{mk}_X(v.\\operatorname{sb}(v,m,a)).\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $i:\\mathsf P_X$, define $z_i:V$ by the following data lambda. At $z:Z$, pack along the even path $g\\to J$ the data function $$\\lambda^{\\mathsf d,\\mathsf d}w:\n             \\operatorname{Single}(z^\\downarrow).\n \\operatorname{send}_n\n    \\bigl(\\operatorname{Access}\n          (w;o,y.i@\\operatorname{lift}_o(y))\\bigr),$$ where all channel types and the lift in its body use $A=z^\\downarrow$. This defines the body of $\\lambda^{\\mathsf d,\\mathsf d}z:Z.\\,\\cdots$. The variable $o$ is the data parameter supplied by $\\operatorname{Access}$. Thus $z_i$ has no free $A,m$. Put $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\operatorname{le}(i,x)&=[x z_i],\\\\\n x\\simeq x'&=\\forall i.\\,(\\operatorname{le}(i,x)\\leftrightarrow \\operatorname{le}(i,x')),\\\\\n \\operatorname{Valid}(x)&=\\forall i,i'.\\,\n       \\bigl(i\\approx i'\\Rightarrow\n                    (\\operatorname{le}(i,x)\\leftrightarrow \\operatorname{le}(i',x))\\bigr),\\\\\n \\operatorname{Ext}(i)&=\\forall x,x'.\\,\n       \\bigl(x\\simeq x'\\Rightarrow(i@x\\leftrightarrow i@x')\\bigr),\\\\\n \\operatorname{Resp}(v)&=\\forall y,z.\\,\n       \\bigl(y\\sim z\\Rightarrow\n                    (v\\diamond_m y\\leftrightarrow v\\diamond_m z)\\bigr).\n\\end{aligned}$$",

      "interpretation": "Lifts and uniform probes define predecessor observations, point validity, predicate extensionality and probe responsiveness without making data depend on proof inputs.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rel-specialized-object",
          "exact_quote": "At $A_0$, for $P:\\mathsf P_L$, define $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal P_G(P)&=\\forall y,z.\\,\n  \\bigl(\\operatorname{Ae}(y,z)\\Rightarrow(P@y\\leftrightarrow P@z)\\bigr),\\\\\n \\mathcal P_{C_t}(P)&=\\exists x.\\forall w.\\,\n                    (P@w\\leftrightarrow\\operatorname{Ae}(w,x^t)),\\\\\n \\mathcal P_{E_t}(P)&=\\exists x.\\forall w.\\,\n       \\bigl(P@w\\leftrightarrow\n        (\\operatorname{Ae}(w,x^0)\\lor\\operatorname{Ae}(w,x^t))\\bigr),\\\\\n i_P&=\\operatorname{pred}_X(x.P@(x^\\ell)),\\\\\n \\mathcal P_R(P)&=\\operatorname{Ext}(i_P)\\Rightarrow\n     \\forall x.\\bigl(\\operatorname{Valid}(x)\\Rightarrow\n                    (P@(x^r)\\Rightarrow \\operatorname{le}(i_P,x))\\bigr).\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $C_t$ has $t=0,\\ell,r$ and $E_t$ has $t=\\ell,r$. Define $$m_0=\\operatorname{mk}_M\n   \\left(bp.\\bigvee_{t\\ {\\rm structural}}\n       \\bigl(\\operatorname{Mark}_t(bp)\\land\n                       \\mathcal P_t(\\operatorname{Dec}(bp))\\bigr)\\right).$$ The formulas $\\mathcal P_t$ use $z_i$, tags, and observations already defined independently of $m_0$; this is a data term with no recursive occurrence of itself.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "At $A_0$, for $P:\\mathsf P_L$, define $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal P_G(P)&=\\forall y,z.\\,\n  \\bigl(\\operatorname{Ae}(y,z)\\Rightarrow(P@y\\leftrightarrow P@z)\\bigr),\\\\\n \\mathcal P_{C_t}(P)&=\\exists x.\\forall w.\\,\n                    (P@w\\leftrightarrow\\operatorname{Ae}(w,x^t)),\\\\\n \\mathcal P_{E_t}(P)&=\\exists x.\\forall w.\\,\n       \\bigl(P@w\\leftrightarrow\n        (\\operatorname{Ae}(w,x^0)\\lor\\operatorname{Ae}(w,x^t))\\bigr),\\\\\n i_P&=\\operatorname{pred}_X(x.P@(x^\\ell)),\\\\\n \\mathcal P_R(P)&=\\operatorname{Ext}(i_P)\\Rightarrow\n     \\forall x.\\bigl(\\operatorname{Valid}(x)\\Rightarrow\n                    (P@(x^r)\\Rightarrow \\operatorname{le}(i_P,x))\\bigr).\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $C_t$ has $t=0,\\ell,r$ and $E_t$ has $t=\\ell,r$. Define $$m_0=\\operatorname{mk}_M\n   \\left(bp.\\bigvee_{t\\ {\\rm structural}}\n       \\bigl(\\operatorname{Mark}_t(bp)\\land\n                       \\mathcal P_t(\\operatorname{Dec}(bp))\\bigr)\\right).$$ The formulas $\\mathcal P_t$ use $z_i$, tags, and observations already defined independently of $m_0$; this is a data term with no recursive occurrence of itself.",

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-uniform-evaluation-stage-lower-bound | F_t(k) | ≥ | $k+1$ | The bound depends on the fixed tree and the environment minus bound, uniformly over typed images and parameters. |
| q-plain-target-potential | p_C(T_q) | ≤ | $p_C(E)$ | The potential counts selected nodes of normalized type syntax; the bound concerns same-component targets. |
| q-plain-child-strict-potential-drop | p_C(E) | < | $p_C(T)$ | The positive integer potential is defined by the selected finite syntax nodes. |
| q-application-evaluation-stage-bound | Application evaluation bound | = | $$$1+\max\bigl\{k,f(k),n(k), f(\max\{k,n(k)\}),n(\max\{k,f(k)\})\bigr\}. \label{obs:bound-application-formula}$$ No monotonicity of $f$ or $n$ is needed: the displayed arguments are the precise ones used above. A plus active function can only have a free plus active continuation, and a minus one only a free minus active continuation, so the cases prove the required output assertion as well as comparison.$ | The displayed bound combines the precise child-bound arguments used in the proof and does not assume monotonicity of the child bounds. |
| q-default-evaluation-stage-bound | Default evaluation bound | = | $k+1$ | Sort defaults and skipped-application defaults, relative to an environment minus bound. |
