<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-validity-thinning** [claim](#stmt-pts-thinning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pts-thinning): Induction on typing derivations, using declaration validity and fresh binder renaming. -> Typing is preserved by valid insertion, including under binders.
- **step-substitution-context-conversion** [claim](#stmt-pts-substitution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pts-substitution): Rule induction with fresh binder extensions, followed by an identity substitution for declaration conversion. -> Simultaneous and dependent-suffix substitution, and replacement of convertible declaration types.
- **step-generation-correctness** [claim](#stmt-pts-generation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pts-generation): Strip final conversion and weakening steps and inspect the introducing rule; prove correctness by rule induction. -> Constructor-specific typing premises, typability of children and correctness of expected types.
- **step-raw-confluence** [claim](#stmt-pts-confluence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pts-confluence): Parallel contraction, parallel substitution and complete developments. -> Confluence, product compatibility and separation of products and sorts.
- **step-preservation-neutral-normal-forms** [claim](#stmt-pts-subject-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pts-subject-reduction): Typing induction for reduction positions, neutral-spine induction and confluence of normalizing reductions. -> Exact-type subject reduction, neutral type uniqueness and normalized judgments.
- **step-finite-restriction-saturation** [claim](#stmt-pts-finite-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pts-finite-restriction): Restrict a hypothetical counterexample derivation and its conversion witnesses to their finite specification support; schedule sorted declarations in a common context. -> A finite specification suffices, and finite valid contexts embed into the saturated context.
- **step-profile-generation** [claim](#stmt-lem-normal-profiles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-normal-profiles): Normal-syntax induction, generation and neutral uniqueness; combine normal witnesses by thinning to realize product outputs. -> Profile formulas, structural invariance, substitution growth and the generated family of feasible profiles.
- **step-tests-head-expansion-candidates** [claim](#stmt-lem-candidate-lattice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-candidate-lattice): Use tested-stack concatenation, induction on reduction heights and intersections of candidates. -> Elementary test properties, strong-normalization head expansion, a complete candidate lattice and product-test candidates.
- **step-fundamental-transport** [claim](#stmt-lem-sn-fundamental) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sn-fundamental): Lexicographic induction on reduction height and annotated syntax size, using fresh-variable extensions, head expansion and specialization. -> The substituted term belongs to the candidate of its normalized expected type.
- **step-advance-and-complete-interface** [claim](#stmt-prop-advance-component) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-advance-component): Apply fundamental transport separately to stack components and exclude a least-size nonnormalizing application. -> Advance the processed invariant; interfaces at all components imply system-wide strong normalization.
- **step-signed-fixed-point-interface** [claim](#stmt-prop-signed-component) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-signed-component): Color vertices by path parity and apply the complete-lattice fixed-point argument in the mixed inclusion order. -> The candidate interface for a sign-consistent component.
- **step-support-bit-interface** [claim](#stmt-prop-one-child-component) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-one-child-component): Recurse along the unique internal product child, track support polarity and compare candidates after substitution using extremal bits. -> Support invariance, directional substitution comparison and the bit-parametrized candidate interface.
- **step-secondary-graph-plain-spaces** [claim](#stmt-obs-plain-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-obs-plain-construction): Propagate graph signs, use tables on finite frame paths and recurse on the decreasing selected-node potential. -> Plain observation spaces with the required prefix decomposition and distinguished values.
- **step-free-observation-spaces** [claim](#stmt-obs-bound-statement) / [proof](#obs:free-uniform-factorization): Construct alternating table stages, their set colimit, restriction maps and uniformly factoring minus behaviors. -> Free observation spaces with uniform-stage factorization and prefix slicing.
- **step-evaluation-bounds-invariance** [claim](#stmt-obs-bound-statement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-obs-bound-statement): Simultaneous syntax-size induction for value evaluation and candidate reading, with uniform environment comparison bounds. -> Total evaluation, uniform finite semantic-stage bounds and structural invariance.
- **step-goodness-substitution-transport** [claim](#stmt-obs-substitution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-obs-substitution): Induct in evaluation order, inspect skipped variable-headed clauses and transport typing trees along full beta reductions. -> Normal-tree goodness, semantic substitution and reduction preservation, followed by normal-tree independence.
- **step-observation-interface** [claim](#stmt-obs-interface-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-obs-interface-theorem): Read candidates on normal syntax and choose the normal argument's value as a tested payload; substitute and transport to the normalized codomain. -> The complete observation-based candidate interface for the remaining components.
- **step-labelled-calculus** [claim](#stmt-lem-enc-labelled-metatheory) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-enc-labelled-metatheory): Repeat structural derivation inductions with mode preservation and use labelled product compatibility to lift erased redexes. -> Labelled metatheory, erasure typing and lifting of every ordinary typed beta step.
- **step-terminals-logic-data-wrappers** [claim](#stmt-lem-enc-data-cancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-enc-data-cancellation): Build finite terminal converters and path-indexed proof and data wrappers; derive propositional proof terms by truth-table cases and cancellation by path induction. -> Finite terminal transformations, derived classical formula operations and exact-input data cancellation proofs.
- **step-channel-paths-rounding-bits** [claim](#stmt-chan-double-laws) / [proof](#proof-stmt-chan-double-laws): Choose parity-controlled paths in the assumed configuration and apply wrapper cancellation to channel rounding and paired bit embeddings. -> Required channel paths, rounding evaluation and signed-bit recovery.
- **step-markers-points-access** [claim](#stmt-chan-access) / [proof](#proof-stmt-chan-access): Discriminate fixed marker strings, decode exact predicate observations and perform the bundle's successive Boolean selections. -> Marker and decoder laws, point-query laws and exact-parameter bundle access.
- **step-generic-relations-lifts** [claim](#stmt-rel-lift-properties) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rel-lift-properties): Construct observational equivalence builders, transport class and pairing observations under admissibility and use exact-parameter access for uniform probes. -> Relation congruence, pairing congruence, lift validity and rounded or guarded exact probe evaluations.
- **step-specialization-admissibility** [claim](#stmt-rel-query-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rel-query-realization): Build separated tagged copies and the nonrecursive query object, then prove property respect and decode its marked queries. -> Tagged observation laws, structural query realization and the specialized admissibility proof.
- **step-specialized-equivalence-pairing** [claim](#stmt-rel-pair-laws) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rel-pair-laws): Identify coded and observational equivalence, extract class and edge witnesses, and separate incompatible tags in pairing evaluations. -> Coded equivalence, class and edge witnesses, and the left response and right point pairing laws.
- **step-response-shift-guards** [claim](#stmt-rel-shift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rel-shift): Represent responsive probes by predicates and chain exact pairing, lift and query comparisons with explicit validity transport. -> Represented response predicates, valid shifts, extensional shifted predicates and the guarded predecessor comparison.
- **step-induction-object** [claim](#stmt-rel-wf) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rel-wf): Transport guarded induction along predicate equivalence and evaluate the proof-independent induction object using rounded probes. -> Induction congruence, validity of the induction object and its guarded induction builder.
- **step-guarded-diagonal** [claim](#stmt-rel-contradiction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rel-contradiction): Construct the negative predicate, prove its extensionality and induction with explicit shift guards, and apply the induction builder at the induction object. -> A proof-mode terminal inhabitant in the fixed ambient labelled context.
- **step-normal-erasure-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-lem-enc-no-normal-proof) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-enc-no-normal-proof): Minimal syntax-size descent through a proof lambda's positive telescope or a converter spine's first proof argument. -> No proof-mode terminal or converter-suffix inhabitant can have normal erasure.
- **step-weak-normalization-graph-exclusion** [claim](#stmt-prop-enc-exclusion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-enc-exclusion): Erase the diagonal terminal proof, normalize it by the system-wide hypothesis and lift that reduction to contradict the normal-erasure obstruction. -> The forbidden configuration is excluded in every remaining primary component.
- **step-main-component-completion** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-enc-exclusion-2): Reduce to a finite specification, select the applicable component interface in dependency order and advance the processed invariant. -> Strong beta normalization of every legal expression in every valid context, including annotation reductions.

          "source_id": "PM-245.1"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-support-dependence",
          "exact_quote": "For fixed typed actual images, the candidate of an $S$ type depends on the bits of its starting context only through its support, if any. Valid thinning and context conversion preserve the support, its polarity, and the candidates. Pointwise convertible actual images preserve the candidates when old bits agree.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-support-substitution",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma,y:B,\\Delta$ be valid, let $\\Gamma\\vdash N:B$, and let $T$ be a normal type of profile in $S$ in that context. Put $$\\Gamma'=\\Gamma,\\Delta[y:=N],\\qquad\n                         T'=(T[y:=N])^\\#.$$ Take typed substitutions of the two contexts into $\\Omega$ whose images are compatible with composition: the pre-substitution image of $y$ is convertible to the actual image of $N$, and the other images agree up to conversion. Equip the substitutions with bits $\\theta,\\theta'$. Fix a comparison sign $\\delta\\in\\{+,-\\}$ and assume:*\n\n1.  *the bits agree on any variable supported by both $T$ and $T'$;*\n\n2.  *if $y$ supports $T$ with polarity $\\varepsilon$, then its pre-substitution bit is $0$ when $\\delta\\varepsilon=+$ and $1$ when $\\delta\\varepsilon=-$.*\n\n*Then, at their common actual normal type, $$R_T(\\xi,\\theta)\n                  \\preceq_\\delta R_{T'}(\\xi',\\theta').$$",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-one-child-component",
          "exact_quote": "If no profile triple has all three vertices in $S$, then the bit-parametrized candidates above implement the candidate interface.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-support-bit-interface",
      "inputs": [
        "No all-component profile triple",
        "Support dependence",
        "Shared-support bit agreement"
      ],
      "method": "Recurse along the unique internal product child, track support polarity and compare candidates after substitution using extremal bits.",
      "output": "Support invariance, directional substitution comparison and the bit-parametrized candidate interface.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-one-child-component"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-obs-graph-lemma",
          "exact_quote": "An active predecessor of a plain vertex is plain. Along an edge between free vertices, the sign is preserved on a positive edge and reversed on a negative edge.*\n\n*Let $T=\\Pi x:D.E$ be a normalized actual type, put $I=\\pi(D)$, $J=\\pi(E)$, and $K=\\pi(T)$, and let $q\\in\\operatorname{Ty}(D)$. Put $T_q=(E[q/x])^\\#$ and $L=\\pi(T_q)$. If $K,L$ are active, then $J$ is active, and $I$ is active whenever $I\\in H$. If instead $K,L\\in S$, then $J\\in S$ and every active $I$ is direct.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-obs-plain-construction",
          "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#obs:forbidden\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"obs:forbidden\">[obs:forbidden]</a>, the plain spaces admit <a href=\"#obs:plain-prefix\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"obs:plain-prefix\">[obs:plain-prefix]</a>. They also admit distinguished values whose bases are $\\mathbf t$ and whose next values are distinguished values.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        }
      ],

        "Active secondary graph",
        "Graph exclusion",
        "Normal type profiles"

    },

          "exact_quote": "#### Free observation spaces\n\nThe construction in this subsection is purely set-theoretic.",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-obs-bound-statement",
      "depends_on": [

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-obs-bound-statement",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed finite tree $t$ there are bounds for each of its $\\operatorname{Val}$ and $\\operatorname{Read}$ evaluations, denoted below by $F_t(k)$ when the evaluation is understood, with $F_t(k)\\geq k+1$, satisfying the following assertions.*\n\n1.  *Every evaluation is defined for every typed environment.*\n\n2.  *In any environment minus-bounded by $k$, a free minus $\\operatorname{Val}$ output belongs to $C_{F_t(k)}$.*\n\n3.  *For two environments minus-bounded by $k$ and agreeing to level $F_t(k)$, plain and minus $\\operatorname{Val}$ outputs are equal, $\\operatorname{Read}$ outputs are equal, and plus $\\operatorname{Val}$ outputs are $\\equiv_k$-equivalent.*\n\n*The output spaces and their indices in the two environments agree: pointwise convertibility of the images gives the same normalized actual types, expression profiles, normalized argument keys, and plain type tags. Increasing a chosen bound preserves all three assertions.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-obs-structural-invariance",
          "exact_quote": "Compatible environments give identical Val and Read outputs. Valid thinning and context conversion, with transported trees and compatible old environments, preserve both outputs. Retargeting a root to a convertible expected type preserves Val. All these operations commute with capture-avoiding renaming of generic declared and bound variables, reindexing the substitution and parameters while leaving their actual images and values unchanged.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-evaluation-bounds-invariance",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite stored typing trees",
        "Plain and free observation spaces",

      "method": "Simultaneous syntax-size induction for value evaluation and candidate reading, with uniform environment comparison bounds.",

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-obs-bound-statement"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-obs-substitution",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-obs-goodness-basic",
          "exact_quote": "Goodness has the structural invariances of Lemma <a href=\"#obs:structural-invariance\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"obs:structural-invariance\">29</a>. If the actual expression profile and $\\{s\\}$ are in $S$ and $\\operatorname{Ax}(s)$ is active, then Read$(M:s)$ is always the base projection <a href=\"#obs:read-base\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"obs:read-base\">[obs:read-base]</a>, at every syntactic shape, and its goodness is equivalent to Val goodness.*\n\n*Every normal syntax tree is good for active Val, under every typed raw substitution. A normal tree $M:s$ is good for Read whenever its generic expression profile belongs to $U\\cup S$.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-obs-substitution",
          "exact_quote": "Let a tree over $\\Gamma,x:D,\\Delta$ be given, and let $u$ be a tree of $\\Gamma\\vdash N:D$. Substitute $u$ capture-avoidably at occurrences of $x$, transporting the context, expectations, and generation data. Give the resulting context a typed environment, and give the original context a compatible environment whose image of $x$ is convertible to the actual expression $N\\xi$. Require the remaining corresponding parameters to agree.*\n\n*Let $I=\\pi((D\\xi)^\\#)$. Assume that $I$ is active whenever $I\\in H$. If $I$ is active, assume Val$(u;\\xi)$ is good and use its value as the original parameter of $x$. Then a good Val or Read evaluation before substitution is good after substitution, and the two outputs are equal.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-obs-reduction-transport",
          "exact_quote": "A one-step full beta reduction of a typed expression transports its tree to a tree of the reduct at the same expected type. For every environment, good Val and Read evaluations remain good and their outputs are preserved. This includes reductions in annotations.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-obs-normal-independence",
          "exact_quote": "On a fixed normal expression in a fixed generic context, Val is independent of its stored tree whenever the root expectations are convertible. Read is independent of the stored tree and of its chosen literal expected sort.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        }

      "output": "Normal-tree goodness, semantic substitution and reduction preservation, followed by normal-tree independence.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-obs-substitution"

          "exact_quote": "Assume system-wide weak normalization and <a href=\"#obs:forbidden\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"obs:forbidden\">[obs:forbidden]</a>. Then <a href=\"#obs:R-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"obs:R-definition\">[obs:R-definition]</a> gives candidates with the required weakening and context-conversion invariance and nonempty parameter extensions. It has the product test clause and the specialization property: if $T=\\Pi x:D.E$ is generic normalized of profile in $S$, $\\Gamma\\vdash N:D$, and $N\\xi$ passes its domain test, then the test of a candidate member $h$ entails $$h\\,(N\\xi)\\in R_{(E[N/x])^\\#}(\\xi,\\theta).$$ The ordinary typed-SN clause applies when the instantiated actual profile lies in $U$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-enc-labelled-metatheory",
          "exact_quote": "The labelled typing rules have context validity, correctness of types, weakening, typed substitution by terms of the declared modes, context conversion, generation, and subject reduction at the exact expected type. Labelled beta reduction is confluent, and convertible labelled products have the same pair of labels and convertible corresponding components. Erasing a labelled typing derivation gives a typing derivation in the original pure type system.*\n\n*If $\\Gamma\\vdash M:A$ is a labelled judgment and $|M|\\longrightarrow_\\beta N$, there is a labelled $M'$ with $$M\\longrightarrow_\\beta M',\\qquad |M'|=N,\n \\qquad \\Gamma\\vdash M':A,$$ up to alpha conversion. The mode of $M'$ is the mode of $M$. Consequently, every finite reduction of $|M|$ lifts to a typed labelled reduction. If $|M|$ is weakly normalizing, some labelled reduct of $M$ has beta-normal erasure.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-enc-terminal-converters",
          "exact_quote": "There is a finite valid extension $\\Delta=\\Delta_{\\mathsf d},\n\\Delta_{\\mathsf p}$ and, for every pair of terminals $F,F'$, a proof term $\\tau_{F,F'}(p):F'$ in $\\Delta,p:F$. Its suffixes are sorted, and its type contains no free proof variables.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-enc-propositional",
          "exact_quote": "If formulas $P_1,\\ldots,P_m$ propositionally entail a formula $R$, there is a specified finite term $\\mathsf{pl}_R(p_1,\\ldots,p_m):R$ under $p_i:P_i$. Here $\\bot$ has truth value false; specified Boolean connectives are parsed, and other subformulas, including quantifiers, may be treated as atoms.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-enc-data-cancellation",
          "exact_quote": "For an even data path there is a proof of $$\\operatorname{use}_\\rho(\\operatorname{pack}_\\rho(t);x.\\Phi)\n                             \\leftrightarrow\\Phi[x:=t],$$ and for an odd data path there is a proof of $$\\langle\\operatorname{mk}_\\rho(x.\\Phi),t\\rangle_\\rho\n                             \\leftrightarrow\\Phi[x:=t].$$ These are uniform term constructors in all the displayed open data parameters. The right-hand sides contain the original input $t$.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        }

        },

          "exact_quote": "There are proof builders, for $y:L$ and $t:B(A)$, of $$\\langle\\operatorname{Round}(y),t\\rangle_L\n       \\leftrightarrow\\langle y,t\\rangle_L.\n\\label{chan-primitive-round}$$ For every formula $\\Phi$ under $x:L$ and every $w:L$, there is a proof of $$\\operatorname{pred}(x.\\Phi)@w\n       \\leftrightarrow\\Phi[x:=\\operatorname{Round}(w)].\n\\label{chan-predicate-evaluation}$$",

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-chan-marker-laws",
          "exact_quote": "For $P:\\mathsf P_L$ and $t,u\\in\\mathcal T$ there are proofs of $$\\operatorname{Mark}_t(\\operatorname{Enc}_t(P)),\\qquad\n \\neg\\operatorname{Mark}_u(\\operatorname{Enc}_t(P))\\quad(u\\ne t).$$ For every $c:L_c$ there is a proof of $$\\langle\\operatorname{Dec}(\\operatorname{Enc}_t(P)),c\\rangle_{\\mathsf P_L}\n       \\leftrightarrow\\langle P,c\\rangle_{\\mathsf P_L},$$ and consequently, for every $y:L$, a proof of $$\\operatorname{Dec}(\\operatorname{Enc}_t(P))@y\\leftrightarrow P@y.\n\\label{chan-decode}$$",

        },
        {

          "anchor": "stmt-rel-equivalence",
          "exact_quote": "The relation $\\sim$ has reflexivity, symmetry, and transitivity builders. There is a builder $\\operatorname{RoundRel}(y):y\\sim\\operatorname{Round}y$. For $e:a\\sim a'$ and $f:c\\sim c'$ there is a builder $$\\operatorname{cong}(e,f):\n       (a\\sim c)\\leftrightarrow(a'\\sim c').$$ These builders require no admissibility assumption.",

          "anchor": "stmt-rel-pair-congruence",
          "exact_quote": "There are unrounded evaluation proofs $$c_y@w\\leftrightarrow(w\\sim y),\\qquad\n u_{yz}@w\\leftrightarrow((w\\sim y)\\lor(w\\sim z)).$$ Equivalent parameters give $c_y\\approx c_{y'}$ and $u_{yz}\\approx u_{y'z'}$. Under $ad:\\operatorname{Adm}$, the predicates $\\operatorname{Is}_t,T_t$ respect $\\sim$. Moreover, still under $ad$, and under $P\\approx P'$, $a\\sim a'$, and $z\\sim z'$ there are proofs of $$\\operatorname{form}(P,a,z)\\leftrightarrow\n       \\operatorname{form}(P',a',z'),$$ and, under the first two of these assumptions, of $\\operatorname{Pair}(P,a)\\approx\\operatorname{Pair}(P',a')$. In particular, under $ad$ there is $$\\operatorname{pv}(P,a,z):\n  \\operatorname{Pair}(P,a)@z\n       \\leftrightarrow\\operatorname{form}(P,a,z).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rel-lift-properties",
          "exact_quote": "The relation $\\simeq$ is an equivalence relation, with the congruence builder of Lemma <a href=\"#rel-equivalence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rel-equivalence\">45</a>, and $$\\operatorname{RoundRel}_X(x):x\\simeq\\operatorname{Round}_Xx.$$ Data cancellation supplies $$\\operatorname{LP}(i,a):\n       \\operatorname{le}(i,\\operatorname{lift}_m a)\\leftrightarrow \\operatorname{sb}(z_i,m,a).$$ Under $ad:\\operatorname{Adm}$ there are builders $$\\begin{aligned}\n z_i\\diamond_m y&\\leftrightarrow\n             i@\\operatorname{lift}_m(\\operatorname{Round}y),\n                  \\label{rel-probe-rounded}\\\\\n \\operatorname{LC}(e)&:\n       \\operatorname{lift}_m(a)\\simeq\\operatorname{lift}_m(a')\n                    \\qquad(e:a\\sim a'), \\label{rel-lift-congruence}\\\\\n &\\operatorname{Valid}(\\operatorname{lift}_m a). \\label{rel-lift-valid}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If also $ex:\\operatorname{Ext}(i)$, then $$z_i\\diamond_m y\\leftrightarrow i@\\operatorname{lift}_m y\n \\quad\\hbox{and}\\quad \\operatorname{Resp}(z_i).\n \\label{rel-probe-exact}$$ If $h:\\operatorname{Resp}(v)$, then $$\\operatorname{Part}(v)@y\\leftrightarrow v\\diamond_m y.\n \\label{rel-part-exact}$$",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-system-wide-normalization**: System-wide weak beta normalization implies strong beta normalization for every legal expression in every valid context, without functionality assumptions on axioms or product rules.
- **invariant-exact-type-preservation**: Full beta reduction preserves the exact original expected type, including when reduction occurs inside annotations.
- **invariant-local-candidate-contract**: The candidate argument uses containment and head expansion; reduction closure is not part of its candidate definition.
- **invariant-uniform-observation-bounds**: For a fixed finite typing tree, evaluation bounds are uniform over typed environments. A representing stage for a minus behavior must work across all observations.
- **invariant-guarded-predecessor-comparison**: The shift predecessor comparison requires an extensional predicate and a valid point. The data operations themselves are defined independently of those proof inputs.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-isolated-term-termination**: Infer strong normalization of an individual expression merely because it has a normal form. -> The premise supplies weak normalization only for that expression, whereas the theorem can use normal forms of other legal expressions in other valid contexts.
- **anti-erasing-annotations**: Treat termination of the computational body after deleting type annotations as sufficient evidence. -> Reductions inside annotations remain part of the reduction relation and can continue independently of the body.
- **anti-general-type-uniqueness**: Implement the profile argument by selecting a unique sort or type for every expression. -> Nonfunctional axioms can give a sort distinct nonconvertible types; uniqueness established for neutral expressions does not extend to arbitrary syntax.
- **anti-finite-stage-runtime-bound**: Read a finite observation-stage bound as an effective upper bound on normalization time. -> The bound controls semantic dependence on observation payloads; the evaluation construction already uses normal forms of varying typed images.
- **anti-unguarded-relational-transport**: Use the predecessor comparison at an arbitrary point or with a predicate lacking extensionality. -> The response comparison and transport between predicate observations lack their required proof inputs.
- **anti-logical-equivalence-as-data-equality**: Replace a wrapped datum inside a function because its observations are logically equivalent. -> Cancellation constructs proofs of formula equivalence and does not authorize substitution of logically equivalent data expressions.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result quantifies over every legal expression in every valid context of an arbitrary pure type system. Its proof constructs candidates over collections of typed expressions and uses set-theoretic existence rather than a finite decision procedure for this universal claim. The saturated-context proof explicitly states: "This is a set-theoretic construction and does not require a decision procedure for typing." The signed-component proof also states: "Tarski’s fixed-point theorem (Tarski 1955, Theorem 1)" and obtains a fixed point on a complete lattice of candidates. Finite tests of syntax or profile graphs would not certify the system-wide normalization implication.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The normalization theorem supplies a conditional metatheoretic guarantee for pure type systems, but does not construct a software evaluator or establish its system-wide weak-normalization premise. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The reduction relation concerns annotated typed expressions. The paper supplies no construction connecting game execution or simulation dynamics to legal expressions of a weakly normalizing pure type system. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The saturated-context construction explicitly relies on set-theoretic typing conditions rather than a typing decision procedure, so it does not provide an executable general type-checking tool. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | For a finite specification under the section's system-wide weak-normalization hypothesis, feasible profiles have a finite closure construction. Starting with the nonempty axiom profiles and their specified singleton generators, repeatedly adjoining nonempty product outputs constructs the family characterized by the lemma. This finite graph preparation does not compute the normalization candidates. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The paper constructs finite typing trees that retain the selected generation witnesses at each syntax node. These trees provide a concrete representation for transporting derivations through structural operations and typed reductions even when sort assignments and formation rules are nonunique. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The observation-space construction uses unrestricted set-theoretic tables and finite-stage representation arguments. It supplies no finite hardware representation or accelerator construction for those spaces. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The labelled proof construction preserves typing, proof/data modes, and erased beta reductions. Those properties do not construct a cryptographic primitive or establish a security property against an adversary. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The candidate product tests quantify over typed strongly normalizing arguments and semantic candidates. The paper provides no learning procedure, statistical objective, or construction relating those tests to a trained model. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The mixed-order fixed point establishes existence of normalization candidates in a complete lattice. It does not construct an optimisation objective, an executable solver, or a finite convergence guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The system-wide normalization property concerns reduction of an expression, while explicitly leaving external context declarations unexpanded. The paper supplies no construction for communicating processes, message schedules, or distributed protocols. |
| other (Mathematical logic and type theory) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | Inside the derived labelled formula calculus, the propositional lemma constructs finite proof terms from a supplied propositional entailment. Its proof explicitly branches over the finitely many atoms and builds the corresponding typed logical terms, providing a concrete proof-construction contract distinct from the nonconstructive normalization argument. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-system-wide-premise** (hypothesis): The normalization implication requires weak normalization of every legal expression in every valid context. A normal form for the expression under study alone does not satisfy this premise. Blocked use: ['Infer strong normalization of an isolated term solely from its weak normalization.', 'Replace the universal premise with successful evaluation of selected closed examples.']
- **limit-full-annotated-reduction** (formal_scope): Strong normalization includes reductions inside type annotations and both components of dependent products. Termination of the body alone does not establish the required property. Blocked use: ['Certify normalization after deleting annotations.', 'Ignore reduction in abstraction domains or product codomains.']
- **limit-beta-only** (formal_scope): The theorem concerns beta reduction. Extension to eta reduction or additional computational rules requires a separate argument. Blocked use: ['Apply the conclusion unchanged to a calculus with additional reduction rules.']
- **limit-valid-contexts-and-typed-substitution** (hypothesis): Context insertion must preserve the original declarations and their order in a valid target context. Substitution must supply images typed at the corresponding substituted declaration types. Blocked use: ['Insert arbitrary unsorted declarations into a typing context.', 'Transport typing through a substitution without checking its dependent declaration obligations.']
- **limit-neutral-only-type-uniqueness** (formal_scope): General type uniqueness is unavailable in nonfunctional specifications. The uniqueness result used by the proof applies to neutral expressions. Blocked use: ['Choose a unique sort for every expression.', 'Extend neutral type uniqueness to sorts or arbitrary expressions.']
- **limit-finite-restriction-is-not-bounded-testing** (counterexample_scope): Finite restriction preserves a hypothetical counterexample by retaining its derivation and conversion witnesses. It does not replace universal normalization with bounded exploration of expressions. Blocked use: ['Treat successful testing of a finite collection of terms as proof of system-wide weak normalization.', 'Assume finite sort and rule sets imply a finite collection of legal expressions.']
- **limit-saturation-not-typing-procedure** (nonconstructive): The saturated context is a set-theoretic construction whose declaration schedule uses typing existence. The construction does not require a typing decision procedure. Blocked use: ['Present the saturated context as an executable general typing algorithm.', 'Assume each scheduled typing condition can be decided.']
- **limit-profile-closure-regime** (hypothesis): The characterization of feasible profiles is developed for a finite specification under system-wide weak normalization. Constructing the finite profile closure does not establish that normalization premise. Blocked use: ['Use profile closure alone to decide weak normalization.', 'Apply the stated feasible-profile characterization outside its section hypotheses without further proof.']
- **limit-candidates-not-reduction-closed** (formal_scope): Candidates have the specified containment and typed head-expansion properties. Reduction closure is not a required candidate property, and head expansion requires strong normalization of all displayed components. Blocked use: ['Invoke arbitrary reduction closure of a candidate.', 'Infer strong normalization of a head expansion while omitting its annotation or argument obligations.']
- **limit-complete-component-interface** (hypothesis): Component advancement requires the processed invariant and the complete candidate interface. Product equality must support both introduction and elimination, together with specialization. Blocked use: ['Advance the processed invariant after proving only an elimination test.', 'Apply fundamental transport without an admissible environment or strong normalization of the source term.']
- **limit-component-branch-conditions** (hypothesis): The signed construction requires absence of an odd closed walk. The support construction requires absence of a profile triple entirely within the component. Neither condition may be dropped when selecting that construction. Blocked use: ['Use the signed fixed-point construction on an arbitrary signed component.', 'Use the single internal child recursion when both product children can remain in the component.']
- **limit-fixed-point-not-finite-iteration** (nonconstructive): The signed-component proof obtains a fixed point in a complete lattice of candidates. Its existence argument does not supply a finite candidate-enumeration procedure or a stopping bound. Blocked use: ['Treat the candidate fixed point as a finite iterative solver.', 'Claim normalization runtime bounds from fixed-point existence.']
- **limit-observation-exclusion-and-uniformity** (hypothesis): The observation interface assumes system-wide weak normalization and the graph exclusion. A minus behavior needs a common representing stage across all observations; choosing a separate stage for each stack is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Use the observation interface before proving its graph exclusion.', 'Replace uniform representability with separate finite-stage witnesses for individual observations.', 'Materialize the unrestricted observation spaces as though they were finite tables.']
- **limit-semantic-stage-not-runtime** (formal_scope): Uniform evaluation bounds concern semantic stages and environment comparisons for a fixed typing tree. They do not bound the reductions used to normalize varying raw images. Blocked use: ['Publish a semantic stage bound as a normalization time bound.', 'Infer a finite implementation memory budget from observation-stage representability.']
- **limit-goodness-for-semantic-transport** (hypothesis): Semantic substitution requires good evaluations, compatible actual images, agreement of corresponding parameters, and the stated active-layer condition. Arbitrary stored trees do not automatically commute with substitution. Blocked use: ['Transport semantic outputs through arbitrary substitution without goodness.', 'Ignore the possibility that an inactive default hides syntax exposed by substitution.']
- **limit-logical-cancellation-not-data-equality** (formal_scope): Wrapper cancellation supplies logical biconditionals with the original input retained by exact substitution. It does not authorize replacing data subterms inside functions by observationally equivalent data. Blocked use: ['Treat a cancellation biconditional as definitional equality of data.', 'Replace an encoded datum inside an arbitrary function without a corresponding congruence proof.']
- **limit-access-and-relational-guards** (hypothesis): Bundle access uses the negative dummy observation as a proof input. Exact probe evaluation requires extensionality, response comparisons require responsiveness, and the shift predecessor comparison additionally requires validity of the original point. Blocked use: ['Use bundle access without its negative dummy proof.', 'Remove rounding from a probe evaluation without extensionality.', 'Apply the predecessor comparison to an arbitrary point without its validity proof.']
- **limit-permitted-logical-domains** (formal_scope): Logical quantifiers use the fixed direct-domain annotations supplied by the construction. Open data parameters are inputs to term builders and are not automatically additional logical quantifier domains. Blocked use: ['Introduce a logical quantifier over an arbitrary channel type without its supporting product rules.', 'Reselect wrapper paths after substitution changes a profile.']
- **limit-proof-mode-normal-erasure-obstruction** (counterexample_scope): The normal-erasure obstruction applies to proof-mode inhabitants of the restricted terminal and converter targets in the specified ambient context and its legal data extensions. It does not exclude data inhabitants of those targets. Blocked use: ['Claim that terminals have no inhabitants of any mode.', 'Apply the obstruction to an unrelated context containing unrestricted proof declarations.']
- **limit-erasure-retains-annotations** (formal_scope): Reduction lifting concerns the mode-forgetting erasure of a labelled typing derivation. Applications, binders, and annotations remain present, and the lifted reduction preserves the term's mode. Blocked use: ['Use annotation deletion as the erasure operation in the exclusion proof.', 'Assume an arbitrary untyped erased step lifts without the labelled typing premise.']

          "exact_quote": "The labelled typing rules have context validity, correctness of types, weakening, typed substitution by terms of the declared modes, context conversion, generation, and subject reduction at the exact expected type. Labelled beta reduction is confluent, and convertible labelled products have the same pair of labels and convertible corresponding components. Erasing a labelled typing derivation gives a typing derivation in the original pure type system.*\n\n*If $\\Gamma\\vdash M:A$ is a labelled judgment and $|M|\\longrightarrow_\\beta N$, there is a labelled $M'$ with $$M\\longrightarrow_\\beta M',\\qquad |M'|=N,\n \\qquad \\Gamma\\vdash M':A,$$ up to alpha conversion. The mode of $M'$ is the mode of $M$. Consequently, every finite reduction of $|M|$ lifts to a typed labelled reduction. If $|M|$ is weakly normalizing, some labelled reduct of $M$ has beta-normal erasure.",
          "source_id": "PM-245.1"
        }
      ],

    }

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 245 (`lean/docs/245.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Weak and strong normalization in pure type systems](../../preprints/Weak-and-strong-normalization-in-pure-type-systems-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves the $\beta$-Barendregt–Geuvers–Klop conjecture for pure type systems: if every legal expression in every valid context has some terminating $\beta$-reduction sequence, then every $\beta$-reduction sequence from every such expression terminates. Reduction is allowed inside type annotations, and the specification may have arbitrary sorts and nonfunctional axioms or rules.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `Expr` | inductive |
| `liftRen` | def |
| `rename` | def |
| `liftSub` | def |
| `subst` | def |
| `single` | def |
| `instantiate` | def |
| `Specification` | structure |
| `Beta` | inductive |
| `Converts` | def |
| `Normal` | def |
| `WeaklyNormalizing` | def |
| `StronglyNormalizing` | def |
| `HasType` | inductive |
| `ValidContext` | inductive |
| `Legal` | def |
| `SystemWeaklyNormalizing` | def |
| `SystemStronglyNormalizing` | def |
| `weak_implies_strong` | theorem |

Comparator source `TypeSystemNormalization.lean` (4493 bytes, sha256 `9e17d5b15e6437488e6337fff8556d9cba5c07108fb551b90d1423603e85ac5d`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-!
Full annotated PTS syntax, modulo alpha-renaming via de Bruijn indices.
Contexts are stored newest-first, each declaration in its prefix context.
No functionality assumptions on axioms or product rules.
-/

namespace PureTypeSystem

universe u

inductive Expr (Sorts : Type u) : Type u
  | var : Nat → Expr Sorts
  | sort : Sorts → Expr Sorts
  | app : Expr Sorts → Expr Sorts → Expr Sorts
  | lam : Expr Sorts → Expr Sorts → Expr Sorts
  | pi : Expr Sorts → Expr Sorts → Expr Sorts
  deriving DecidableEq

namespace Expr

variable {S : Type u}

def liftRen (ρ : Nat → Nat) : Nat → Nat
  | 0 => 0
  | n + 1 => ρ n + 1

def rename (ρ : Nat → Nat) : Expr S → Expr S
  | var n => var (ρ n)
  | sort s => sort s
  | app f a => app (rename ρ f) (rename ρ a)
  | lam A b => lam (rename ρ A) (rename (liftRen ρ) b)
  | pi A B => pi (rename ρ A) (rename (liftRen ρ) B)

def liftSub (σ : Nat → Expr S) : Nat → Expr S
  | 0 => var 0
  | n + 1 => rename Nat.succ (σ n)

def subst (σ : Nat → Expr S) : Expr S → Expr S
  | var n => σ n
  | sort s => sort s
  | app f a => app (subst σ f) (subst σ a)
  | lam A b => lam (subst σ A) (subst (liftSub σ) b)
  | pi A B => pi (subst σ A) (subst (liftSub σ) B)

def single (a : Expr S) : Nat → Expr S
  | 0 => a
  | n + 1 => var n

def instantiate (b a : Expr S) : Expr S := subst (single a) b

end Expr

structure Specification (S : Type u) where
  axioms : S → S → Prop
  rule : S → S → S → Prop

inductive Beta {S : Type u} : Expr S → Expr S → Prop
  | head (A b a) : Beta (.app (.lam A b) a) (b.instantiate a)
  | app_left {f f' a} : Beta f f' → Beta (.app f a) (.app f' a)
  | app_right {f a a'} : Beta a a' → Beta (.app f a) (.app f a')
  | lam_domain {A A' b} : Beta A A' → Beta (.lam A b) (.lam A' b)
  | lam_body {A b b'} : Beta b b' → Beta (.lam A b) (.lam A b')
  | pi_domain {A A' B} : Beta A A' → Beta (.pi A B) (.pi A' B)
  | pi_body {A B B'} : Beta B B' → Beta (.pi A B) (.pi A B')

def Converts {S : Type u} : Expr S → Expr S → Prop := Relation.EqvGen Beta

def Normal {S : Type u} (M : Expr S) : Prop := ∀ N, ¬ Beta M N

def WeaklyNormalizing {S : Type u} (M : Expr S) : Prop :=
  ∃ N, Relation.ReflTransGen Beta M N ∧ Normal N

/-- Accessibility for the converse of reduction: strong normalization,
including reductions in all type annotations. -/
def StronglyNormalizing {S : Type u} (M : Expr S) : Prop :=
  Acc (fun N M : Expr S => Beta M N) M

inductive HasType {S : Type u} (P : Specification S) :
    List (Expr S) → Expr S → Expr S → Prop
  | ax {s t} : P.axioms s t → HasType P [] (.sort s) (.sort t)
  | var {Γ A s} : HasType P Γ A (.sort s) →
      HasType P (A :: Γ) (.var 0) (A.rename Nat.succ)
  | weaken {Γ M B A s} : HasType P Γ M B → HasType P Γ A (.sort s) →
      HasType P (A :: Γ) (M.rename Nat.succ) (B.rename Nat.succ)
  | product {Γ A B s₁ s₂ s₃} : HasType P Γ A (.sort s₁) →
      HasType P (A :: Γ) B (.sort s₂) → P.rule s₁ s₂ s₃ →
      HasType P Γ (.pi A B) (.sort s₃)
  | abstraction {Γ A b B s} : HasType P (A :: Γ) b B →
      HasType P Γ (.pi A B) (.sort s) →
      HasType P Γ (.lam A b) (.pi A B)
  | application {Γ f a A B} : HasType P Γ f (.pi A B) →
      HasType P Γ a A → HasType P Γ (.app f a) (B.instantiate a)
  | conversion {Γ M A B s} : HasType P Γ M A → HasType P Γ B (.sort s) →
      Converts A B → HasType P Γ M B

inductive ValidContext {S : Type u} (P : Specification S) : List (Expr S) → Prop
  | nil : ValidContext P []
  | cons {Γ A s} : ValidContext P Γ → HasType P Γ A (.sort s) →
      ValidContext P (A :: Γ)

def Legal {S : Type u} (P : Specification S) (Γ : List (Expr S)) (M : Expr S) : Prop :=
  ∃ A, HasType P Γ M A ∨ HasType P Γ A M

def SystemWeaklyNormalizing {S : Type u} (P : Specification S) : Prop :=
  ∀ Γ, ValidContext P Γ → ∀ M, Legal P Γ M → WeaklyNormalizing M

def SystemStronglyNormalizing {S : Type u} (P : Specification S) : Prop :=
  ∀ Γ, ValidContext P Γ → ∀ M, Legal P Γ M → StronglyNormalizing M

/-- System-wide weak normalization implies strong normalization for every PTS,
including arbitrary sort types, nonfunctional rules and annotated terms. -/
theorem weak_implies_strong {S : Type u} (P : Specification S)
    (h : SystemWeaklyNormalizing P) : SystemStronglyNormalizing P := by
  sorry

end PureTypeSystem

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every weakly <i>β</i>-normalizing pure type system is strongly <i>β</i>-normalizing. Both properties quantify over all legal expressions in all valid contexts, and reduction acts inside type annotations. No functionality hypothesis is required. This resolves the <i>β</i>-Barendregt–Geuvers–Klop conjecture.

| [stmt-rel-shift](#stmt-rel-shift) | lemma | `rel-shift` | 54 | Define the data operations |
| [stmt-rel-ind-congruence](#stmt-rel-ind-congruence) | lemma | `rel-ind-congruence` | 55 | For $d:i\approx i'$ there is a builder |

| [stmt-rel-contradiction](#stmt-rel-contradiction) | proposition | `rel-contradiction` | 57 | The specialized relational construction yields a proof of $\bot=F_b$ in the ambient labelled context.* |
| [stmt-rel-diagonal-dependencies](#stmt-rel-diagonal-dependencies) | remark | `rel-diagonal-dependencies` | 2 | At the fixed specialization, $i_v$ is defined before its response and extensionality proofs; $\delta$ and $S$ are data definitions independent of those proofs; $\operatorname{WF}$ uses the already def |
| [stmt-lem-enc-no-normal-proof](#stmt-lem-enc-no-normal-proof) | lemma | `lem:enc-no-normal-proof` | 58 | In the ambient context above, or in any legal extension by data declarations, there is no proof-mode term $P$ with beta-normal erasure and a judgment $P:T$ for any $T\in\mathcal T$.* |
